# Lists: features and interface

Status: partly implemented, version 0.1.0. What exactly is done and how it is checked is in the section "What is implemented" at the end.
Sources: the request of the author of the project of 2026-10-05; the list of 2Do features from its documentation (links in [architecture.md](../architecture.md)).

## Purpose and scope

A personal task list for one person on several devices: macOS and Android now, iOS later. The model is 2Do, but with fewer entities and actions.

The interface language is English, with a Russian localisation; the app follows the system language.

Out of scope: shared lists, location reminders, "call/message" actions, duration estimates, groups of lists and groups of tags, a smart list builder, sync with Dropbox and Apple Reminders.

## Requirements

### Lists

- R1. A task belongs to one list. The "Inbox" list always exists; it cannot be deleted, but it can be hidden (R121); everything without a list goes there, except new tasks and import while "Inbox" is hidden (R40, R121).
- R2. A list has these settings: name, colour, icon, sorting (manual, by due date, by priority, by name), showing completed tasks, defaults for new tasks (priority, due date "today" or empty). The settings sync.
- R3. Lists are ordered manually; a list can be archived and restored.
- R136. macOS, Android and the web interface: deleting a list asks for confirmation (#319, #330): macOS and Android ask "Delete the list “Work” and move its tasks to the trash?" with the name of the list, the web interface asks "Delete the list and move its tasks to the trash?". The list is deleted only after "Delete"; "Cancel", Esc and, on Android, Back change nothing; on Android the settings of the list stay open after a refusal. Deleting a list cannot be undone (R131): its tasks are restored from the trash one by one.
- R121. macOS and Android: "Inbox" can be hidden (#255). The "Show Inbox" switch is in the settings, in the "General" section: on macOS in the "Sidebar" group (R93), on Android as a row of its own. The flag is shared by all devices and syncs (S32 in [sync.md](sync.md)). Turning it off opens a dialog with a choice of a list among those not archived. If there are no such lists, the dialog says "To hide Inbox, create a list first.", and the switch stays on. If "Inbox" has open tasks, the dialog names their number and offers two buttons: "Move and hide" and "Hide without moving". Moving sends the open top-level tasks and the open projects of "Inbox", together with their subtasks, to the end of the chosen list; completed tasks stay where they are. Without open tasks there is one button, "Hide". "Cancel" leaves everything as it was. Hiding sets the default list for new tasks (R40) to "last used", and the list chosen in the dialog becomes the last used list on this device. If "Inbox" is open at that moment, the app goes to the chosen list. A single undo (⌘Z on macOS, "Undo" in the snackbar on Android, where the settings close for it) returns the moved tasks to "Inbox", shows "Inbox" again and restores the previous choice of R40. While "Inbox" is hidden, it has no row either in the macOS sidebar or in the Android panel of lists, except while it holds open tasks. Such tasks arrive by sync from a device the flag has not reached yet, from the "Inbox" calendar over CalDAV, or after "Hide without moving"; the row is then shown with a counter and disappears when no open tasks are left in "Inbox". Tasks from "Inbox" are never moved anywhere silently. Import puts tasks without a list into the default list (R40). Turning the switch on returns the row and does not change the choice of R40. The web interface shows "Inbox" as before.

### Task

- R4. Fields: name, note, start date, due date, priority, tags, repeat, reminder, attachments, subtasks. Only the name is required.
- R5. The start date and the due date are a date or a date with a time, without a time zone: "tomorrow at 9:00" stays nine in the morning after a flight. A task with a start date in the future is hidden from "Today" until that date.
- R6. Priority: none, low, medium, high.
- R7. Tags are free-form and are created while typing. A tag without tasks disappears from the sidebar.
- R8. A reminder is a point in time; the platform shell shows a local notification.
- R36. Notifications are set on each device separately and do not sync: whether to show them at all; how long before a due date with a time to remind about the task (at the due time, 5 minutes before … a day before; several options can be chosen at once, and then several notifications come for the task; until the person has chosen, there is one reminder, at the due time); at what hour to remind about a task due on a day; whether to send a daily summary and when. A reminder set on the task itself replaces the one derived from the due date and is always shown while notifications are on. A notification about a task shows its name and due date: "Due: today", "Due: tomorrow, 18:30", "Due: Fri, Oct 9". The day of the due date is named as it looks at the moment the notification appears, not at the moment the notifications were scheduled: a task due today whose reminder was scheduled the day before comes with the word "today" (#280). The notification sound is also chosen on each device. On macOS the sound is turned on and off; when it is on, the standard notification sound or one of the system alert sounds is chosen, and the chosen system sound plays at once. On Android the "Sound" row in the notification settings opens the system settings of the "Reminders" channel: since Android 8 the sound of a channel belongs to the person, and the app cannot change it after the channel is created. Before the version with #206 the default was "15 minutes before", and the apps wrote this value on any save of the notification settings, so a saved "15 minutes" cannot be told from the old default: the update turns a saved choice of exactly "15 minutes before" into "at the due time" once. Any other saved choice, including "15 minutes" together with another option, and "off", stays; "15 minutes before" chosen after the update also stays.
- R111. macOS: when the system does not allow Lists to show notifications, the settings, in the "Notifications" section (R101), show under the "Show notifications" switch the line "macOS does not allow notifications for Lists." with the button "Open System Settings…"; the button opens the notifications of Lists in System Settings. While there is no permission, no notification from R36 is passed to the system. The answer of the system is read at launch, every time the app comes to the foreground, and after every request for permission; a refusal is written to the log (R102) together with the error text of the system. When permission appears, the line goes away, and the schedule of notifications is passed to the system at once, without waiting for the next change of tasks. While the app has never asked for permission, there is no line: the system asks by itself when the first notification appears. On Android permission is covered by R66 and R104.
- R103. macOS: the app icon in the Dock shows a number of tasks. What is counted is chosen in the settings, in the "Notifications" section (R101): "Today" counts the open tasks of the "Today" view together with overdue ones (R84); "Overdue tasks" counts open tasks whose due date is before today; "Do not show". The default is "Today". When there is nothing to count, the icon has no number: zero is not shown. While Lists is removed from the Dock (R116), there is no number. The number follows the data: it changes after every change of tasks in the app, after a change that came from the quick entry window, the Share extension or sync, and when the day changes, also while the main window is closed or the app is hidden. A change of the choice in the settings applies at once. The number does not depend on the "Show notifications" switch (R36). The setting is stored on the device and does not sync. On Android the number on the icon is described by R106; the web interface has no number.
- R104. Android: in the settings, in the "Notifications" section (R101), a persistent notification for quickly adding a task is turned on; it is off by default and does not depend on the "Show notifications" switch (R36). The notification shows the open tasks of the "Today" view in the order of that view, overdue ones first (R84): the title "Today: N" (or, when the icon counts overdue tasks and there are some, "Overdue: N", R106), under it the names of the first five tasks, each on a new line, and "N more" when there are more tasks; when there are no tasks, "Nothing for today". Completed tasks that stay in view (R68) are not included. The "New task" button opens the quick entry window (R25, R42); tapping the notification itself opens the app. The content follows the data: it changes after a change of tasks in the app, after a sync that brought changes, and when the day changes. The update for the change of day comes right after midnight when the app is allowed "Alarms & reminders" (R66); without this permission the system may delay it by up to an hour. This is the only persistent notification of the app: there is no separate notification with just a number (#200), and it also carries the number on the icon (R106). The notification sits in one of two low-importance channels, without sound or vibration: "Quick add, with the count on the icon" while the icon has something to show, and "Quick add" the rest of the time; whether a channel gives a badge is decided when it is created, and a notification in the channel with a badge marks the launcher icon at any number. Both channels are hidden in the system settings separately from "Reminders". The notification is persistent: "Clear all" does not remove it, and a swiped-away one (Android 14 and later) comes back at once; only the switch in the settings removes it. After the device restarts and after the app updates, it appears again without the app being launched. When there is no permission for notifications (Android 13 and later), turning the setting on requests it; without permission there is no notification, and it appears after permission is given. Whether the names are visible on the lock screen is decided by the system settings. The setting is stored on the device and does not sync.
- R106. Android: the app icon on the home screen shows a number of tasks. What is counted is chosen in the settings, in the "Notifications" section (R101), with the same options as on macOS (R103): "Today", "Overdue tasks", "Do not show". The default is "Do not show". Android takes the badge on a launcher icon only from a notification of the app; the number is carried by the persistent notification with the tasks for today (R104), and there is no separate notification with just a number (#200). So the number is there only while that notification is on: when it is off and a number is chosen, a caption under the choice says that the notification has to be turned on. The title of the notification and the number agree: with "Today" chosen, both say how many open tasks are in "Today"; with "Overdue tasks" chosen, how many of them are overdue, and when none are overdue, the icon has no number and the title stays "Today: N". At zero and with "Do not show" chosen, the launcher icon has no badge. How the badge looks is decided by the home screen launcher: some show the number on the icon, others show a dot and the number in the long-press menu of the icon; the app does nothing beyond that. The number follows the data and the change of day together with the notification (R104). A change of the choice in the settings applies at once. The number does not depend on the "Show notifications" switch (R36). Update from a version where the number had its own notification: that notification and its "Count on the icon" channel are removed; for someone who had only the number turned on, the notification with tasks is turned on once so that the number does not disappear, and it can be turned off later as usual. A reminder that sits in the notification shade is counted by the launcher itself and added to the number. The setting is stored on the device and does not sync.
- R9. A deleted task goes to the trash and is restored from there; the trash is emptied manually.

### Subtasks

- R10. A subtask is a line with a mark and a name, and nothing else: it has no note, dates, repeat, reminder, priority, tags, attachments or subtasks of its own. A subtask is added by typing its name under the task; it is renamed, completed, closed as "won't do" (R69), reordered among the subtasks of the same task, and deleted. A task does not turn into a subtask, and a subtask does not turn into a task; a subtask is not moved to another parent or to another list. The exception is a project (R30): its tasks stay full tasks, and a task of a project can have subtasks by the same rule. There are no separate "project" and "checklist" types.
- R11. Completing a task completes its incomplete subtasks. Returning a task to work does not touch the subtasks. Undoing a completion (R131) undoes the whole action: the task and the subtasks completed together with it return to work; subtasks completed earlier stay completed.
- R12. Withdrawn (#205). A subtask is never shown as a row of its own: not in "Today", not in "Upcoming", not in the view of a tag or a filter; search shows the task it belongs to (R22).
- R13. Withdrawn (#205): a subtask is not moved and does not become standalone (R10).
- R112. Data that does not fit R10 is brought into line with it on each device: at launch, at the end of a sync that has read everything in the storage (S42 in `sync.md`), after import and after a restore from a backup. A subtask loses its note, start date, due date, repeat, reminder, priority, tags and attachments, in the data and not only on screen. A subtask of a subtask moves up a level: it becomes a subtask of the same task as its former parent and stands right after it. Tasks of a project (R30) are not affected. Before existing data is brought into line for the first time, a backup is made by R92. A device with an earlier version can write a field to a subtask again; the field is removed when the change arrives.

### Projects

- R30. A project is a top-level task with the "project" flag; there is no separate entity. Its subtasks are the tasks of the project, full tasks with all fields (R10). A project is shown in the sidebar with a mark (R126) and a "done/total" counter, and opens as a view of its own: open tasks, then completed ones. A "won't do" task (R69) is not part of the counter.
- R31. A task entered in the view of a project becomes its subtask; the quick entry line is parsed as usual. Tasks of a project with dates, including a due date taken from the project (R125), are shown in "Today" and "Upcoming" with the name of the project.
- R125. The dates of a project apply to its tasks (#264). A task of a project without its own due date gets the due date of the project; a task with its own due date keeps it, even if it is later than the due date of the project. The start of the project is a lower bound for a task with dates: a task that has its own start or due date (its own or the project's) starts no earlier than the project, so its start is the later of its own start and the start of the project. For a task without its own dates in a project without a due date, the start of the project gives no dates: the task stays without dates. Such a due date and start are effective: by them the task stands in "Today" and "Upcoming" (R21, R5), counters and overdue status are computed, a filter selects it (R33), views are ordered, and by the effective due date a reminder comes and the daily summary is computed (R36); the summary does not count the project itself, its due date shows there in its tasks. Before the start of the project the task is not in "Today", and it stands in "Upcoming" like a task with its own future start. Effective dates are computed for display and are not written to the data: a task stores only its own start and due date, a change of the project's dates moves its tasks at once, and sync over WebDAV and CalDAV stores and sends the task's own dates as they are. The card of a task shows and edits only its own dates. The row (R72) shows the effective due date; a due date taken from the project stands in the same place in a muted colour with the project icon before it, an overdue one in red, and the icon stays. The rule is the same on macOS, Android and in the web interface. It does not apply to subtasks (R10): they have no dates.
- R32. A completed project leaves the sidebar. A project that has tasks does not turn back into a task (#205): its tasks would become subtasks and lose their fields. Tasks closed as "won't do" and tasks in the trash are its tasks too. An empty project becomes a task again. The action is where the project is shown (R120): on macOS in the context menu of the project in the sidebar, on Android in the long-press menu of the project in the navigation drawer, in the web interface as the "Turn back into a task" button above the tasks in the view of the project, after which the list that holds the task opens (#266). A project with tasks has neither the menu item nor the button. A project is not moved into another project, and a task with subtasks is not moved into a project.
- R126. The row of a project in the sidebar is a big task (#274). Instead of an icon it has a round mark in the colour of the project's list, filled by the share of completed tasks "done/total" (R30); the counter stays in place. Tapping the mark completes the project whatever the state of its tasks: open tasks of the project and their subtasks are completed together with it, like subtasks by R11, and a repeating task of the project is completed for good, without moving on to the next repeat. The same "Complete project" item is in the context menu of the project on macOS and in the long-press menu on Android, on any project, next to "Turn back into a task" on an empty one (R32); in the web interface, where the sidebar has no menu, it is a button above the tasks in the view of the project. A completed project leaves the panel (R32); if its view was open, "Inbox" opens. A single undo (⌘Z on macOS, "Undo" in the snackbar on Android and on the web) returns to work the project and the tasks completed together with it; tasks completed earlier stay completed. The snackbar with undo on Android and on the web always appears, with any setting of R68: the row of the project leaves the panel at once.
- R127. A project is created directly, not only from an existing task (#279). On macOS there is the "New project" item in the "+ New List" menu at the bottom of the sidebar, the "+" button in the heading of the "Projects" section and the "New Project…" item in the "File" menu; on Android the "New project" row under the projects in the navigation drawer; in the web interface the "+ New project" button under the projects in the sidebar. The "Projects" section is shown even without projects, like "Filters". All paths open one dialog with one field, the name of the project. The list of the project is not asked: it is the list on screen; in the view of a project, the list of that project; where the view has no list of its own, the default list for new tasks (R40). The list defaults for new tasks (R2), priority and "due today", are not given to a project: the due date of the project would become the due date of all its tasks (R125). A new project has no dates, tags or priority; after it is created, its view opens.
- R129. A project is chosen wherever a list is chosen for a task (#293): in the card of a new task and in the quick entry window (R82, R64), in "Move to list" for one task and for selected ones (R117, R109, R118), and where the list of an open task is changed: on Android with the "Move to list" item in the menu of the card, in the web interface with the "Move to" field. Projects stand under the lists as a separate group "Projects", in the same order as in the sidebar; projects from archived lists are not in it, just as those lists are not. A project chosen for a task makes it a task of the project: it moves to the project's list and stands at the end of the project. A new task for which a project is chosen is created in it the same way as one entered in its view (R31), together with the subtasks typed in the card (R113). Choosing a list for a task of a project is an ordinary move: the task leaves the project and becomes a top-level task of that list, including the list where the project itself stands. These are not moved into a project: the project itself, a task that already stands in it, a task with subtasks and a subtask, as when dropped on a project (R123). For one task such items are unavailable: for a project and for a task with subtasks the whole "Projects" group is unavailable, for a task of a project its own project. For selected tasks such tasks are skipped and the rest are moved. Undo is the same as for "Move to list": the task returns to the previous list or to the previous project and stands there first. In the view of a project the card of a new task shows this project in place of the list, and it is changed to a list or to another project. The quick entry line does not recognise a project: `@name` chooses only a list (R23). The new task field in the web interface has no list choice, so a project cannot be chosen there either.
- R120. A project is shown only in the sidebar; it does not stand as a task row (#254). It is not in the list, in "Inbox", "All", "Today", "Upcoming", in the view of a tag or in a filter, whether open, completed or closed as "won't do", including a list that shows completed tasks (R2) and a filter with any status. The project's own dates, tags and priority do not bring it back there: "Today" and "Upcoming" hold only the tasks of the project with dates, including dates taken from the project (R31, R125). The counters of lists, "Inbox", "All", "Today", "Upcoming", overdue, tags and filters do not count the project. A project stays a row where the sidebar does not show it or does not help: in "Completed" and "Won't do" (a completed project leaves the panel, R32), in the trash so that it can be restored, and in search, which finds a project by its name. Turning a task into a project removes its row from the views; an empty project that becomes a task again (R32) returns the row to its former place in the list.

### Repeats

- R14. Presets: every day, on weekdays, every week, every two weeks, every month, every quarter, every year.
- R15. Custom: every N days, weeks, months or years; for weeks, a set of weekdays; for months, a day of the month or the "first … fifth, last" weekday. End: never, after N repeats, on a date. A custom repeat with an end is set in full on macOS, on Android and in the web interface (#374): the rule keeps only the chosen end, and the default end date is one month from today.
- R16. Counting is from the due date or from the completion date.
- R17. On completion a repeating task stays the same record: the due date and the start date move to the next repeat, the subtasks return to work, and a record of the completed repeat is added to "Completed". The record carries the name, list, priority, due date, start date, tags and place of the task in the list as they were before the move, so it stands where the task stood (R68). If the repeat was the last one, the task is completed in the usual way.
- R128. Completing a repeating task is undone (#287) the same way as any action (R131), and also by tapping the mark of the record of the completed repeat while it stands in the place of the task, and in "Completed" (R133). Undo returns everything the completion changed: to the task, its due date, start date, reminder and number of remaining repeats; to the subtasks, their state before the completion: those completed earlier are completed again with the same moment, the rest are open. The record of the repeat leaves "Completed". Undo works only while the task stands on the date this completion moved it to: if since then it was completed again, on this or another device, or its due date or start date was changed, undo changes nothing. Undo is a change of data like any other: it syncs (S44 in [sync.md](sync.md)). The same applies to a repeating task closed as "won't do" (R69).
- R133. In "Completed" and "Won't do" a record of a completed repeat is returned to work by tapping its mark, like an ordinary completed task (#312). Tapping undoes that completion by R128: the task gets back its due date, start date, reminder, number of remaining repeats and the state of its subtasks, and the record leaves the log. The mark is there only while the completion can be undone: the task stands on the date this completion moved it to. A record whose completion can no longer be undone, because the task was completed again since then or its due date or start date was changed, carries a repeat icon instead of a mark, and tapping it does nothing; it looks the same in any other view where it is shown (R68). Returning is the same action as returning an ordinary task to work: it goes into the undo history (R131), and undoing it completes the repeat again. An ordinary task, as before, returns to work in its former place.
- R18. Day 31 in a month without it means the last day of the month.

### Attachments

- R19. Any number of files and images can be attached to a task. An attachment is available offline once it is downloaded to the device.
- R20. The content of attachments syncs separately from the fields of tasks: an edit of text does not wait for a file upload, and a task with an attachment reaches the device and is shown before its content (S34 in [sync.md](sync.md)).
- R76. An attachment whose content has not reached the device yet is shown in the card of the task as pending. Tapping it downloads this file ahead of the queue (S35 in [sync.md](sync.md)) and opens it; while the file is coming, the row shows this, and a failure is shown in the same row and does not prevent trying again. How many attachments are waiting to be transferred to or from the storage is shown by the sync icon (R27) and the sync settings; a failed transfer of an attachment is not counted as a sync error. On the web the server acts as the device: tapping a pending attachment downloads the file from the storage to the server and opens it on the page the same way as other attachments (R56), and the number of pending ones is shown by the sync button. With sync over CalDAV the content arrives together with the task, and tapping an attachment that is not on the server (C5 in [caldav.md](caldav.md)) downloads nothing.
- R54. An image and a PDF open inside the app, offline and without switching to another app. On macOS this is the system preview (Quick Look) on a click on the name and on Space, with the original file name; it also shows everything the system can show, not only images and PDF. Opening in the default app stays in the context menu of the attachment. The keyboard moves to the row of the file while it is shown and stays on it after the preview closes: Space shows the file again, Esc closes the card, and the list keys (arrows, Return, Space, delete) do not apply to the selected row. On Android it is a full-screen viewer: an image zooms with two fingers and with a double tap, a PDF pages through. Attachments of other types on Android open in an external app.
- R55. In the card of a task an image attachment has a thumbnail instead of the generic icon.
- R85. The type of an attachment whose name does not name it (no extension or an unknown extension) is determined from the content of the file when the content is on the device. JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP, HEIC, PDF, MP4, MOV and M4A are recognised. Such an attachment is shown, opened and saved as a file of its type; a name without an extension is shown with the extension ("Image" as "Image.jpg"), a name with an unknown extension does not change. Nothing is rewritten in the data: the name the file came with is stored, and on a device where the content has not arrived yet the attachment is shown under that name. When the name names the type, the name decides.
- R86. An attachment whose content is on the device can be saved outside the app and handed to another app, under the name it is shown with. macOS: an attachment in the card has a "⋯" button with the items "Quick Look", "Open in Default App" and "Save As…"; a right click on the icon and the name of the attachment opens the same items. A right click elsewhere in the card opens the menu of the task. "Save As…" opens the system save dialog with the file name; a file at the chosen place is replaced, and a write error is shown as a message. An attachment is dragged from the card to Finder and to other apps; one dragged back into the card does not become an attachment. Android: an attachment in the card has a "⋮" button with the items "Save to…" (the system choice of place, after writing a short message about success or failure), "Share" (the system share sheet) and "Open in another app"; "Save" and "Share" are also in the in-app viewer of images and PDF.
- R91. All attachments of a task are saved outside the app in one action; it is there when the task has more than one attachment. macOS: the "Save All…" item in the attachment menu (the "⋯" button and a right click on an attachment) and in the menu of the task; it opens the system folder choice and writes each attachment into it under the name it is shown with. Android: in the "⋮" menu of an attachment and in the "⋮" menu of the task, the items "Save all to…" (the system folder choice) and "Share all" (the system share sheet with all the files). Nothing in the folder is replaced: if a name is taken, by a file that was already there or by another attachment of the same task with the same name, the attachment is written under a free name. On macOS the app chooses the name: a number before the extension, starting from two ("Image 2.jpg"); on Android the system document provider does. Attachments whose content has not arrived yet (R76) are downloaded first: the action waits for them, and their rows in the card show the download. An attachment that could not be downloaded or written is named in the message and does not prevent saving the rest. Choosing some of the attachments is not possible: some are saved one by one (R86).
- R56. On the web only PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP and PDF are shown in place; the type is taken from the record of the attachment, and the list is set by the server. Such a file is served with its type, with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and with `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`. SVG, HTML and everything else are never shown and are only downloaded, even if the page asks to show them; what is shown can be downloaded too.
- R140. The size of attachments is limited on each device separately: by default 512 MiB per file and 2 GiB for all live attachments of one task. The limits are stored on the device and do not sync: a phone and a server can have different room to spare. A file over the limit and a file after which the sum of the task's attachments goes over the limit are rejected with an error that names the limit; no attachment appears, and content copied by this call does not stay in the attachments folder. Content is read and written in parts, so the memory of the process does not grow with the size of the file. On macOS and Android adding an attachment and import run off the main thread, and the window does not freeze; the error is shown in the same window. The web server takes its limits from `LISTS_MAX_FILE_MB` and `LISTS_MAX_TASK_MB`; zero in them means "no limit".
- R141. The web server serves an attachment from disk without reading it whole: it answers `Accept-Ranges: bytes`, understands a single range `bytes=…` (including `bytes=N-` and `bytes=-N`) and answers `206` with `Content-Range`. A range past the end of the file, an empty file and a reversed range give `416` with `Content-Range: bytes */<size>`; an unclear or multi-range header is ignored, and the whole file is served. `HEAD` serves the headers of the full file without a body. The type, name, execution ban and `nosniff` of a part of a file are the same as for the full response.
- R142. On Unix the data folder, `blobs/` and `backups/` are accessible only to the owner (`0700`), and the database, the log, the content of attachments and the backup files (R87) are readable and writable only by the owner (`0600`); an existing folder is brought to this when opened, together with the backups made by the previous version (#355). An attachment restored from a backup (R90) gets the same permissions as an added one. A backup saved by the user outside the data folder ("Save As…") gets the permissions of the place they chose. On other systems the system manages permissions.

### Views

- R21. The smart views are fixed: Inbox, Today (due today or earlier, or a start date today or earlier when the due date is empty), Upcoming (by day), All, Completed, Won't do (R69), Trash.
- R46. "Completed" shows the whole log and is cleared manually: everything at once or everything completed before a chosen point (older than a month, older than a year). Clearing deletes for good, bypassing the trash, completed top-level tasks together with their subtasks and records of completed repeats. Tasks and records with the "won't do" outcome (R69) are in the same log and are removed the same way. Open tasks, their completed subtasks and the trash are not affected.
- R47. Replaced by R93: the "Show Completed in the sidebar" setting became one of the cases of the setting for sidebar items. Neither of them affects the content of the log or the showing of completed tasks inside a list (R2).
- R93. macOS: for each built-in view except "Inbox" ("Today", "Upcoming", "All", "Completed", "Won't do", "Trash"), the settings, in the "Sidebar" section, set when it has a row in the sidebar: "Always", "When it holds something" or "Never". Since #255 the row of "Inbox" is governed by the switch of R121, shared by all devices; a "Never" choice for "Inbox" made earlier no longer applies. By default "Today", "Upcoming" and "All" are always shown, "Completed", "Won't do" and "Trash" when they hold something. A Mac on which the former "Show Completed" setting (R47) was off gets "Never" for "Completed" and "Won't do". "Holds something" means the view would show at least one task: for "Completed", "won't do" tasks also count; for "All", open top-level tasks. The setting belongs to the device and does not sync. Lists, tags, projects and filters are not subject to it. When the row of the open view disappears, through the setting or because nothing is left in it, the app goes to "Inbox". The "Go" menu item and the ⌘1–⌘6 key of a view that has no row at the moment do nothing; the item does not turn grey. If "Inbox" itself is hidden (R121), going to "Inbox" when a row disappears stays, and it has no row then.
- R68. A completed task does not leave the view at once. It stays in its place with a mark, a struck-through name (on Android without strike-through, R159) and a muted colour until the set time has passed since completion; tapping the mark during that time returns it to work. The time is chosen in the settings: "at once", 5 seconds, 15 seconds or "until the end of the day"; the default is 5 seconds. "Until the end of the day" means until midnight by the device clock: the task stays in view until the day on which it was completed is over. A time in minutes chosen in a previous version (1, 5, 15 minutes or an hour) keeps working and stays in the choice until it is changed. The setting is one for all devices and syncs (S32 in [sync.md](sync.md)). The count runs from the completion time, not from the tap on this device: a task completed on another device stays in view after sync until the end of the same period, and restarting the app does not extend the period. The period is accurate to the second: it is counted from the moment the completion was recorded. A task whose completion was recorded just now but happened long ago (import, a CalDAV calendar read for the first time) is not treated as delayed: the completion date must match the moment of recording to within a minute. The rule applies in "Inbox", in a list, in "Today", "Upcoming", "All", in the view of a tag and in a filter with the "Open" status; counters do not count such a task as open. In a list that shows completed tasks (R2), in a project (R30) and in a filter with the "All" status, the task does not disappear when the time runs out, but moves to the end, to the other completed tasks. The setting also applies to repeating tasks (#287). A repeating task on completion moves on to the next repeat (R17), stays open and at once stands where its new date puts it, including in the same view. In its former place, for the same time, stands the record of the completed repeat, with a mark, a struck-through name and a muted colour; tapping the mark of the record undoes the completion (R128). The snackbar after completion on Android and on the web appears with any setting; for a repeating task it is a message about the move; it always has "Undo" (R131). ⌘Z on macOS undoes a completion with any setting, including the completion of a repeating task. The app removes such a row by itself, without user action, in the second its time runs out; the web interface does so while the page is open and no text is being typed in it.
- R69. A task can be closed with a second outcome, "won't do": it will not be done, but nobody wants to delete it either. The action is the "Won't do" item in the context menu of a row on macOS, in the long-press menu of a row on Android, a button in the card on the web; only an open task has it. The card of a task on macOS and Android does not have it: the row of fields in the card holds only fields. The square mark, swiping and keys stay for completion. Such a task behaves like a completed one. It stays in view and leaves views by the rules of R68, and tapping the mark returns it to work. Its open subtasks get the same outcome with the same moment; those completed earlier stay completed. Undoing the action returns them to work together with the task (R11). A repeating task moves on to the next repeat by R17, and the record of the repeat in the log is marked "won't do". Its mark is a square with a cross instead of a check; the name is struck through and muted, as for a completed task. It is shown in "Completed" next to completed tasks and in the smart view "Won't do", a read-only selection from the same log. Clearing "Completed" (R46) removes both outcomes; "Won't do" has no clearing of its own. On macOS the view opens with ⌘6 and is removed from the sidebar by its setting (R93). Such a task is not part of the project's "done/total" counter (R30) or of the subtask counter in a row, in either number. In a filter (R33) it has its own status, and "Completed" means only completed tasks. Storage and the behaviour of versions that do not know this outcome are in S33 in [sync.md](sync.md); exchange with other CalDAV clients is in C27 in [caldav.md](caldav.md).
- R22. Search by name and note across all lists. A task is also found by the name of its subtask: the results show the task itself, and under it those of its subtasks whose name contains the search text; a subtask is not a row of its own in the results.
- R33. A saved filter is a named view with conditions: date (any, overdue, today, the next N days together with overdue, no date), lists, tags (all at once), priority not lower than a given one, status (open, completed, won't do, all), words in the name or the note. The date of a task of a project is the effective one (R125). All the conditions set must hold. Ready-made presets: "Next 7 days", "Overdue", "High priority", "No date".
- R34. Filters sync like the rest of the data. Over CalDAV they are stored as a property of the "Inbox" calendar and are not carried over if the server does not store such properties.
- R72. The row of a task in a list on macOS and Android has the same content on both platforms, on Android with the differences of appearance from R159; it is one line of text, and where lists are mixed, two: the name of the list stands under the title. The mark comes first: square, slightly smaller than the height of the text, centred on the height of the row rather than on the first line of text. After the mark comes the priority as a thin vertical bar the full height of the row's text: red for high, orange for medium, blue for low; a task without priority has no bar, but the space for it remains, so the titles of all tasks start on one vertical line. There are no "!" signs in the row. The project icon (R30) stands before the title when there is one. After the title come icons without text or numbers, only those the task has, in one order: repeat, note (present when the note is not empty), attachments, subtasks. In a view where lists are mixed — "Today", "Upcoming", "All", a tag view, search, a filter, "Completed", "Won't do", the Trash — the name of the task's list stands under the task title in small type, in the colour of that list, without an icon; for a task of a project (R31) it is followed by a dash and the name of the project in a muted colour: "List – Project". "Inbox" is such a list too: its task shows "Inbox" in the colour of the app, because "Inbox" has no colour of its own. In the view of one list, including "Inbox", and in the view of a project, the name is not repeated. The task title and the line under it are each truncated to their own width and do not crowd each other. The due date stands at the right edge; the overdue due date of an open task is red and always includes the date. A due date taken from the project (R125) stands in the same place in a muted colour with the project icon before it. When the day of the due date is already named by a group heading — "Today" in the "Today" view, a day in "Upcoming" — the day is not written: the time remains, and a due date without time leaves the right side empty. The start date, tags, the subtask counter, the number of attachments and the reminder are not shown in the row: they are in the card of the task. Rows are spaced more loosely than before. In the web interface the mark and the priority bar are the same, and under the title, as before, stand the due date, the icons and the name of the list; there too the due date of the project is muted and shown with the project icon.
- R84. In "Today" on macOS and Android tasks are divided into the groups "Overdue" and "Today" when there are overdue tasks. An overdue task is an open task whose due date is before today. The start date does not put a task in the "Overdue" group: a task without a due date or with a due date today or later whose start has already passed stands in the "Today" group. The "Overdue" group and the red counter of "Today" count the same tasks.
- R73. The title of a task in a list row on macOS and Android takes one line. A title that does not fit the width is truncated at the end with an ellipsis, and the height of the row does not depend on the length of the title. The full title is in the title field of the card of the task: a title that does not fit the width wraps there onto the following lines and is visible in full; the height of the field is not limited. A title has no line breaks: Return in the field ends editing, as before, and pasted text of several lines becomes one line.
- R78. "Today" on macOS and Android shows the events of the current day from the system calendars: as a block above the tasks, read-only. The setting "Show calendar events in Today" turns this on; by default it is off, belongs to the device and is not synced. The app asks the system for access to the calendars when the setting is turned on, and not before. The system grants access to all calendars at once; which of them to show is chosen in the settings of the app: under the switch stands the list of system calendars with a checkmark at each. The device stores the cleared checkmarks, so a new calendar is shown until it is cleared. When there is no access, there is no block, and the settings say so in a line that opens the system access settings. An event is one line: a bar in the colour of the calendar, the start time, the title. An all-day event and an event that started before today have no time. Events without time come first, then events with time by start time; on a tie, by title. An event that has already ended today stays in the block until the end of the day. The block is muted: a background with rounded corners across the width of the list, grey text smaller than the task title; an event has no mark. An event is not a task: it cannot be completed, changed or moved, and it is not part of the "Today" counter, search or sync. A tap on Android opens the event in the calendar app. A click on macOS opens "Calendar": there is no documented way to open it at the event or at the day, so the app opens as it is. The block is shown also when there are no tasks for today; when there are no events, there is no block. The block is reread when the system calendars change, when the app returns to the screen and when the next day begins. The other views and the web have no events.

### Quick entry

- R23. One line of text becomes a task. Understood are: `#tag`, `@list`, `!`, `!!`, `!!!` for priority, the words «сегодня» (today), «завтра» (tomorrow), «послезавтра» (the day after tomorrow), days of the week, the date `DD.MM` and `DD.MM.YYYY`, the time `HH:MM`; the same in English. What is recognised is removed from the title and shown as chips before saving; each chip can be removed.
- R24. macOS: a global hotkey opens the quick entry window over any app, a second one shows the main window (R124); the menu bar icon opens the same field and the tasks for today, and the icon can be turned off (R116); the "Share" extension accepts text, links, files and images.
- R137. macOS: the "Share" extension writes a task only to the same database that Lists opens, in the app group container (#319). In a build without a signing team there is no group: the form of the extension opens with the message "This build of Lists cannot save from Share: it is signed without a development team", the "Add" button is unavailable, and the extension creates no database. The extension does not attach a file that lies in the Lists data folder or in the group container: this way another app cannot pass the database or attachments of Lists into a task, and with it into the sync storage. Such a file and a file that could not be attached are written by the extension to the log (R102), and the task is created without it.
- R98. macOS: the quick entry window opened by the global hotkey appears alone, whatever state the app is in. When the app is hidden ("Hide Lists", ⌘H), neither the main window nor the settings window comes back together with the quick entry window. After the quick entry window is closed — by saving, by Esc or by switching to another app — Lists is hidden again, and its windows come back when the app is shown: from the Dock, by ⌘Tab or with "Open Lists" in the menu bar. If the person switches to Lists while the quick entry window is open, its windows come back at once. The same applies to the window of the menu bar icon (R24, #292): it appears alone over the hidden app, after it closes Lists is hidden again, and the items "Open Lists" and "Settings…" of this window close it and bring back the windows of the app at once.
- R124. macOS: a second global hotkey shows the main window of Lists over any app (#258). By default it is not set: it is recorded in the settings, on the "Tasks" tab, in the group "Show Lists" under the group "Quick Entry" with the hotkey from R24. Pressing it makes Lists active and brings the main window to the front: a hidden app ("Hide Lists", ⌘H) is shown with its windows, a window minimised to the Dock is restored, a closed one is opened again. When Lists is already active and its main window is in front, the same press hides Lists, as "Hide Lists" does; when the settings window or the quick entry window is in front, the press brings the main window to the front. The quick entry helper (R115) does not listen to this hotkey: while Lists is not running, it does nothing. The hotkey belongs to this Mac and is not synced; Android and the web interface do not have it.
- R115. macOS: Lists and quick entry start with the system, through two switches in the settings; both are off by default. Both are in the "General" section, as the first group "At login", so that they are visible without scrolling. "Open Lists at login": the system starts Lists when the person logs in; the window opens the same way as on a manual start, unless something else is chosen (R116). "Quick entry without opening Lists": at login a helper starts, a separate program inside Lists, without a Dock icon, without a menu bar icon and without windows of its own. The helper listens to the quick entry hotkey (R24) and shows the same quick entry window with the same rules (R82, R114, R58, R113); Lists itself does not need to run for this. The helper turns on as soon as the switch is on and stops as soon as it is off. It opens the same data folder and takes the settings of Lists: the hotkey, the list of a new task, the size and typeface of the text, the appearance, the parsing of the line, the note from the clipboard. While Lists is running, Lists answers the hotkey and the helper releases it; when Lists quits, the helper takes the hotkey again. The helper does not sync and does not schedule notifications: a task written through it is on this Mac at once, and it goes to the storage and gets its reminders when Lists is running; a running Lists is told about the change at once. On each start of Lists the helper is restarted, and this is the first thing Lists does on start, before opening the data folder and before reading the keychain: so a helper left from the previous version after an update does not write into the new data folder with the old core while the start waits, for example, for the person to answer a keychain prompt. The previous helper stops at once, and the start does not wait until the system starts the new one: the system keeps ten seconds between two starts of the same agent. The system starts Lists at login as a login item; it starts the helper as a user launch agent, the file `~/Library/LaunchAgents/org.evsyukov.lists.helper.plist` with the path to the program inside Lists: the place the system reserves for a helper inside an app works only in a build signed by a developer, and in an unsigned build the helper stops there on start. If Lists is moved to another place, the file is rewritten on the next start of Lists. The system stores the state of both switches, and they show what the system reports: Lists removed from System Settings → General → Login Items is shown as off. When the system is waiting for permission from the person, forbids the helper to run in the background or refuses, the switch is off, and under it stand a line with the reason and a button that opens the login items in System Settings. The settings belong to this Mac and are not synced. On Android quick entry without the app is always there (R25); the web interface does not have it.
- R116. macOS: the Lists icon in the menu bar and the icon in the Dock are set in the "General" section, in the group "Menu bar and Dock". "Show Lists in the menu bar" turns the icon from R24 on and off; it is on by default. "Hide Lists from the Dock" removes the app icon from the Dock; it is off by default, and it can be chosen only while the menu bar icon is shown. When the menu bar icon is turned off, the Dock icon comes back at once and "Hide Lists from the Dock" turns off: Lists is never left without both icons. Both settings apply at once, without a restart, and are kept between launches. While Lists is removed from the Dock, it is not among the apps on ⌘Tab and it has no main menu in the menu bar: this is how the system shows an app without a Dock icon; the keyboard shortcuts of the main menu still work while a Lists window is active. The main window is opened by "Open Lists" in the menu bar window (R98), the settings by "Settings…" in the same place; this item is always there. Without a Dock icon there is no task count (R103), and it does not move to the menu bar; the "Count on the icon" choice in the settings is unavailable, under it stands the line "Lists is hidden from the Dock", and the saved choice remains and works again when the icon comes back. In the group "At login" under "Open Lists at login" stands "Show the window at login": it is on by default; when it is off, Lists started by the system at login stays hidden, as after "Hide Lists" (R98), and the window opens when the app is shown; a manual start is not affected. The setting is unavailable while "Open Lists at login" is off. The settings belong to this Mac and are not synced.
- R117. macOS: several tasks of a view are selected the way rows of lists are selected in the system, and are changed by one action. A click with ⌘ adds a row to the selection or removes it; a click with Shift selects all rows from the one selected by a plain click to the one clicked; Shift with the up or down arrow grows and shrinks this range by one row; Option with an arrow goes to the first or the last row, and with Shift as well extends the range to it; ⌘A selects all rows of the view; typing the start of a title while the list has the keyboard selects the next task whose title starts with it, as in the tables of the system. As in the tables of the system, a click selects the row when the button goes down; a plain click on one of several selected rows leaves only it selected when the button goes up without a drag. A plain click, an arrow without Shift and Esc leave one row selected. Any row the view shows can be selected: a task, a subtask, a task of a project, a task in the Trash, a record of a completed repetition. All selected rows lie on the same highlight as a single selected one. While several rows are selected, Return does nothing: the card does not open for several tasks; a double click on a row leaves only that row selected and opens its card. Changing the view and search clear the selection; a row that has left the view drops out of the selection. A view chosen in the sidebar or with ⌘1…⌘6 gets the keyboard: the arrows select its tasks at once rather than move through the sidebar, and its row in the sidebar stays highlighted in the same colour as when the keyboard is in the sidebar; the card of a new task opened at that moment (R37) keeps the keyboard. The context menu of any of the selected rows applies to all selected: "Complete", "Due" and "Start" (today, tomorrow, in a week), "Priority", "Move to list" (lists and projects, R129), "Tag" (existing tags, and "New tag…" with input of a name), "Won't do", "Delete". The menu of a row that is not in the selection stays the menu of that one row. Keys and menu bar items that exist for one task act on all selected: Space and ⌘Return complete, ⌫ deletes, ⌘T and ⇧⌘T set the due date to today and to tomorrow. The action does to each task the same as the action of the same name does to one. A task the action does not apply to is skipped: a closed one for "Complete" and "Won't do"; one that already stands in the chosen list at the top level; one that already has this due date, this start, this priority or this tag; a subtask (R10) for due date, start, priority, tag and moving. Removing the due date, the start or a tag from several tasks at once is not possible. When all selected rows are tasks in the Trash, the menu has one item, "Restore"; when all are records of completed repetitions, one item, "Delete records"; in a mixed selection the usual actions do not apply to a task in the Trash or to a record. One undo (⌘Z) returns each task to its previous state (R131): it reopens closed tasks together with the subtasks closed by the same action, returns a repeating task its previous due date (R128), restores deleted tasks, moves restored tasks back to the Trash, sets the previous due date, start and priority, removes an added tag, and returns a task to its previous list or previous project at its previous place. Dragging selected tasks onto the sidebar is described in R123. On Android selecting several tasks is described in R109.
- R123. macOS: tasks are dragged from a view onto a row of the sidebar (#257). Dragging any of the selected rows (R117) moves all selected ones; a row that is not in the selection becomes the selection when the button goes down, as in the tables of the system, and is moved alone. Next to the pointer are the title of the row being dragged and the number of tasks when there is more than one. Tasks can be dropped on "Inbox" and on a list, which is "Move to list"; on a project, where the tasks become its tasks and go to its end in the order of the view; on "Today", where they get the due date today, as with ⌘T. Other rows of the sidebar do not accept tasks. Skipping and undo are the same as for the context menu action in R117: on a drop onto a project the project itself is skipped, as are a task that already stands in it, a task with subtasks (a project can contain a task with subtasks, but such a task is not moved there) and a subtask; one undo (⌘Z) returns each task to its previous state. A single dragged task follows the same rules. On Android there is no dragging onto the panel of lists: selected tasks are moved with a button of the bar (R109).
- R118. Web interface: several tasks are selected and changed by one action, the same way on a computer and on a phone, through a selection mode, as on Android (R109). In the header of a view that has tasks stands "Select": it turns the mode on while nothing is selected. In the mode the header shows a cross, "Selected: N" and "Done"; the cross and "Done" close the mode. Pressing a row selects it or clears its selection; each row has a circle at the right edge, empty when the row is not selected and filled when it is, and a selected row lies on a pale blue highlight. While the mode is open, rows do not open the card, the mark does not complete the task, and there is no field for a new task. The actions stand in a bar at the bottom, in place of the field for a new task: "Complete", "Due", "Move to list" (lists and projects, R129), "Delete" and "More", which opens "Start", "Priority", "Tag" and "Won't do"; while nothing is selected, they are unavailable. The due date and the start are set as a date without time; a tag is entered by its name. When all selected rows are tasks in the Trash, the bar has one button, "Restore"; when all are records of completed repetitions, one button, "Delete". What the action does to a task, which task it skips and how it is undone are as in R109. The action closes the mode and shows one snackbar with the number of changed tasks — "Completed: N", "Deleted: N", "Restored: N", "Moved: N", "Changed: N" — and "Undo", which in one step returns each task to its previous state; when no task has changed, there is no snackbar. The mode is also closed by changing the view and by search; a task that has left the view drops out of the selection. The server performs the action and its undo: one request per action (`batch`), one per undo (`undo`, R131).
- R25. Android: the app accepts "Share" (text, links, files, images); the "Lists" item in the menu of selected text, the "New task" shortcut on the icon and the tile in Quick Settings open a light input window without starting the main screen.
- R107. Android: home screen widgets with tasks. Two are offered: a small one, 2×2 cells, and a large one, 4×4; the size of each can be changed. When a widget is placed, the person chooses what it shows: "Today", "Inbox" or one of the lists, except archived ones. Each widget has its own choice; it is changed in the widget settings, where the home screen offers them. The header shows the name of the view, and under it the open tasks in the order of that view, for "Today" the overdue ones first (R84); completed tasks that stay visible (R68) are not part of the widget. There are as many rows as fit the height; when there are more tasks, the last row is "N more"; when there are no tasks, "No tasks". The mark stands before the task title: pressing it completes the task the same way as the mark in the app (a repeating task moves on to its next time), and the row leaves the widget; this can be undone only in the app. Pressing the header, a task title or "N more" opens the app in the view of the widget. The widget updates after each change on the device, after a sync that brought changes, after a reboot and at least once every half hour, so that "Today" moves to the new day. The appearance is light or dark following the system. If the list of the widget no longer exists, the header shows "List" and there are no tasks.
- R108. Android: in the header of the widget with tasks (R107) a "+" button stands on the right: pressing it opens the quick entry window (R25, R42). The task goes to the default list of quick entry, whatever the view of the widget.
- R109. Android: several tasks are selected and changed by one action. In the menu of a task opened by a long press, the first item is "Select": it turns on the selection mode, and this task is already selected. Anything the view shows as a row can be selected, including a task of a project shown separately (R31), a task in the Trash and a record of a completed repetition: for them "Select" stands before their only item; the card of the task has no selection mode. In the mode the top bar shows a cross, "Selected: N" and "Done"; the cross, "Done" and "Back" close the mode. Pressing a row selects it or clears its selection. Each row has a circle at the right edge: empty when the row is not selected, filled when it is; a selected row lies on a pale blue highlight. A drag started on a circle, up or down, selects the rows from the first one to the one under the finger; moving back clears the selection of the rows this drag added, and what was selected before it remains. The list does not scroll during the drag. While the mode is open, rows do not open the card, are not swiped and are not marked completed, the task menu does not open, the panel of lists does not open by gesture, pull-to-refresh does not start a sync, and there is no new task button. The actions stand in a floating bar at the bottom: "Complete", "Due", "Move to list" (lists and projects, R129), "Delete" and "More", which holds "Start", "Priority", "Tag" and "Won't do"; while nothing is selected, they are unavailable. When all selected rows are tasks in the Trash, the bar has one button, "Restore"; when all are records of completed repetitions, one button, "Delete". The action does to each selected task the same as the action of the same name does to one task, including to its subtasks. The due date and the start are set the same for all, with the same dialog (R74); removing the due date or the start from several tasks at once is not possible. A tag is added to each; removing a tag from several at once is not possible. A task the action does not apply to is skipped: a task in the Trash for any action except "Restore"; a record of a completed repetition for any except "Delete", so in "Completed" with a mixed selection "Delete" removes both tasks and records, and the other actions skip the records; a closed task for "Complete" and "Won't do"; one that already stands in the chosen list at the top level; one that already has this due date, this start, this priority or this tag. The action closes the mode and shows one snackbar with the number of changed tasks — "Completed: N", "Deleted: N", "Restored: N", "Moved: N", "Changed: N" — and "Undo"; when no task has changed, there is no snackbar. "Undo" in one step returns each task to its previous state (R131): it reopens closed tasks, returns a repeating task its previous due date (R128), restores deleted tasks, moves restored tasks back to the Trash, sets the previous due date, start and priority, removes an added tag, and returns a task to its previous list or previous parent task at its previous place. The mode is also closed by changing the view and by search. A task that sync has removed from the view drops out of the selection; when no selected tasks are left, the mode stays open with zero.
- R139. macOS and Android: if a task could not be created from the card of a new task, the card stays open with everything typed and the chosen files, and the error is shown as a message (#319, #320). On macOS this is the card in the main window, in the quick entry window and in the menu bar window, on Android in the main screen and in the quick entry window; the Android quick entry window does not show "Added" on an error. While the task is being saved, "Save" on Android is unavailable. Nothing written before the error remains in the data: the created task goes to the Trash, as on undo (R131), so saving again does not produce a duplicate. If the task was created but a file could not be attached, the task remains, the error is shown and names the files that were not attached, the card closes, and in the main screen of Android it is cleared for the next task.

### Undo

- R131. Any action that changes a task can be undone on all platforms, last one first (#311). The following are undone: creating a task, a project, a subtask and a copy; the title and the note; the due date, start, reminder, repeat, priority, tags; turning into a project and back; completing and "won't do" under any setting of R68, including for a repeating task (R128); returning to work; deleting and restoring; moving to a list, to a project and to another place in the list; adding and removing an attachment. Undo returns the task to its previous state together with the subtasks and the repeat record that the same action changed. A task created by the action goes to the Trash; a moved one returns to its previous place among its neighbours. An action on several tasks (R109, R117, R118, R123) is undone in one step. Several edits in a row of the title or the note of one task are also one step. Undo does not touch a task that was changed after the action on this or another device, including by sync and by a CalDAV client; it returns the other tasks of the same action. Undo is a change of data like any other: it is synced, and it cannot itself be undone. Each device has its own undo history, kept in the memory of the app; it holds the last 100 steps and is empty after a restart. In the web interface each login session has its own history: undo in one browser does not touch actions done in another. Emptying the Trash and "Completed" (they are irreversible) and actions on lists and filters, except hiding "Inbox" (R121), are not undone. How to undo: on macOS, ⌘Z, and when the focus is in a text field, ⌘Z first undoes typing in it; on Android, "Undo" in the snackbar that appears after each action except editing the title and the note, and the "Undo" item in the menu of the top bar while the history is not empty; in the web interface, "Undo" in the snackbar after each action except editing the title and the note, and Ctrl+Z or ⌘Z when the focus is not in an input field.

### Appearance

- R35. On macOS the text size (five steps) and the typeface (system, rounded, serif, monospaced) are chosen in the settings and apply at once. The steps are factors of the system size: small 1, regular 1.15, large 1.3, larger 1.5, largest 1.7; "Regular" by default (#397). "Regular" is larger than the system size, which reads small in the window of Lists. A factor saved by an earlier version, whose steps were 0.9, 1, 1.15, 1.3 and 1.5, stands for the nearest step: 1.15 is the new "Regular", 0.9 is "Small".
- R100. On Android the text size is chosen in the settings from five steps: small 0.9, normal 1, large 1.15, larger 1.3, largest 1.5; normal by default. They are not the steps of macOS (R35): the system size of Android is already large enough. The step multiplies the font size set in the system: the system setting keeps working, and the step adjusts it only for the app. The choice applies at once, without a restart, to all text of the app: the main screen, cards, dialogs and the quick entry window (R25); icons and spacing do not change. The typeface is not chosen on Android. The setting is stored on the device and is not synced.
- R122. macOS and Android: the spacing between tasks in a list is chosen in the settings, in the "General" section next to the text size (#256): "Compact", "Normal" or "Relaxed"; "Normal" by default. The spacing is the padding above and below the content of a task row. On macOS at the system text size ("Small", R35) a one-line row is 26, 30 and 40 points high respectively: "Normal" is as in Things 3, "Relaxed" is the row height before #256. On Android the padding is 4, 8 and 12 dp, the row 56, 64 and 72 dp (#387): "Normal" is the row height of the R159 appearance; the touch area of the mark does not let the row be lower than 48 dp. On macOS the row height is set by the text and the spacing: the chevron on the right is never taller than the text. The spacing applies to the rows of views, including the tasks of a project in its row; an open card, the subtasks in the card and group headings are not affected. The choice applies at once, is stored on the device and is not synced, like the text size (R35, R100).
- R61. The appearance is chosen on each device: "Same as the system", "Light" or "Dark"; the default is the system's. The choice applies at once to the whole app (on macOS, to the main window, the quick entry window, the menu bar window and the settings) and is kept between launches. The setting belongs to the device, in the web to the browser, and is not synced. The appearance changes the background and the text colour; the colours of lists and the red colour of an overdue due date stay the same (principle 7).
- R101. The settings on macOS and Android are split into five sections. "General": starting at login and quick entry without opening Lists (R115, macOS only), appearance (R61), text (R35, R100), spacing between tasks (R122), sidebar items (R93, macOS only), showing "Inbox" (R121), completed tasks (R68). "Tasks": new tasks, the quick entry hotkey (macOS only), calendar events (R78). "Notifications" (R36). "Sync". "Backups" (R89). The set of settings does not change. On macOS the sections are tabs of the settings window, and the window opens on the tab that was chosen last; what was entered on the "Sync" tab and not saved stays in place while the window is open, whichever tab the person switches to. On Android the settings take the whole screen and open as a list of sections; pressing opens a section, the arrow in the header and "Back" return to the list, and "Back" in the list closes the settings. Under the sections in the list stands "Import…". The "Save and sync" button is only in the "Sync" section; what was entered in it and not saved stays in place while the settings are open. The web interface does not change.
- R102. The apps for macOS and Android keep a log of their work: the file `log.txt` in the data folder of the app, written by the core, the same way on both platforms. The log level is chosen in the settings, in the "General" section (R101): "Off" ("None" on macOS), "Errors", "Events", "Detailed"; "Errors" by default. The level is stored on the device and is not synced. "Errors": a failed sync pass, a failed backup (both the automatic one and the removal of old backups), a failed import, a refusal of the push server at login, a failed transfer of attachment content in the second pass (S34) and in an on-demand download (S35), a failed adding of an attachment, a failed calculation of the notification plan (R36), a push receiving address that could not be saved, and a list of addresses of other devices that could not be read (S19). Failures that only the app code sees are also written as an ERROR line with a source: notify — the system rejected a notification (macOS) or refused permission for notifications (Android); push — the UnifiedPush distributor did not register the app (with the reason) or removed the registration (Android), the push server token was not saved to the keychain (macOS) or was not decrypted (Android); sync — the storage password was not saved to the keychain (macOS) or was not decrypted (Android), the sync settings were not read at start; attachments — a file chosen in the app or passed to it could not be copied or obtained, a file did not open in another app, did not go to "Share", was not copied for Quick Look; widget — a task was not marked completed from the widget (Android); app — the card of a task did not open (Android), the backup settings were not read (Android); login — the helper was not registered again after the app was moved (macOS). On Android the switch of notifications to inexact alarms, without permission for exact ones (R66), is one ERROR line at the switch and an "Events" level line when the permission comes back; the device remembers which state the line was about and does not repeat it on each recalculation. The line names what failed and the reason: the error text of the core, the system error code (domain and number on macOS, exception class on Android) or the reason of the distributor; the text of a system error about a file is not written, because it names the file. A failure to open the database does not get into the log: the core writes the log through the open database. A sync failure that repeats the previous one word for word is written only at the "Detailed" level: a device without a network does not fill the log with the same line. A failed attachment transfer names the direction (upload or download), the folder and the first 8 characters of the hash (`blobs/c2/c2ab12cd…`) and the reason, the error text of the storage with the HTTP code if there is one; the full hash, the file name and the task title are not written. For each attachment the device remembers in its database since when it has not been transferred and when there was last an ERROR line about it; so a restart of the app does not make a failure new. A new failure is an ERROR line at once; the same failure again is a "Detailed" level line, and after an hour an ERROR again with the moment it started ("failing since …"). Content that is not in the storage is first an "Events" level line: another device usually has not uploaded it yet (S34); if it is missing for an hour or longer, an ERROR line with the moment the wait started. An attachment that went through after a failure is an "Events" level line. "Events" adds the course of work: the start of the app with its version, a sync pass with the number of received and sent changes, a created and a restored backup, the result of an import, a change of the log level. "Detailed" adds a pass over attachment content with the number of sent and received files and a sync pass that changed nothing. A log line: local date and time, level, source, message; a line break inside the message is replaced by a space. Titles and notes of tasks, names of lists and tags, passwords and tokens, and the push receiving address with the ntfy topic do not get into the log. The text of a sync error is the same as the settings show and can name the server address. The file does not grow without limit: when it is larger than 1 MB, it becomes `log.1.txt` in place of the previous one, and writing goes to a new `log.txt`. The log is not synced and is not part of backups. In the settings next to the level is an action that hands out the log as a file: "Show in Finder" on macOS, "Share" on Android (both files, if both exist), and "Clear", which deletes both files. While there is no log, it cannot be handed out. The web server writes to its error stream, as before, and does not have this setting.
- R79. macOS and Android: the built-in views in the sidebar and in the panel of lists have a filled icon in a constant colour of its own: "Inbox" blue, "Today" yellow, "Upcoming" pink, "All" turquoise, "Completed" green, "Won't do" and "Trash" grey. The colours are the same on both platforms, differ only in brightness between the light and dark appearance, and depend neither on the colours of lists nor on the Android dynamic palette. The icon of a list stays in the colour of the list; the icons of tags, projects and filters have no colour.
- R80. macOS: the main window has no toolbar strip with a title. The content starts with the large title of the view, and its icon stands before the title: for a built-in view the icon from R79, for a list the icon and colour of the list, for a tag, a project, a filter and search their icon from the sidebar. The "+" button, the sync icon and the search icon stand at the top right of the window, at its right edge. Search is an icon, not a field: a click on it or ⌘F opens the search field to the right of the large title and puts the cursor in it; Esc clears the search and removes the field, and the field is also removed when it is left empty and when the person goes to another view. ⌘N works as before. The window title in the system (the "Window" menu, Mission Control) stays the name of the view.
- R95. Android: the look of the list screen. The top bar has no title: on the left is the button of the panel of lists, on the right the search icon and after it the sync icon. The content starts with the large bold title of the view, with an icon before it: for a built-in view the icon from R79, for a list the icon and colour of the list, for a tag, a project and a filter their icon from the panel of lists. The title scrolls together with the list and stands above an empty view too. While search is open, its field stands in the bar, and there is no large title. The completion mark in a task row is muted and does not carry the colour of the list; its look, the look of a group heading and the look of the new task button are in R159. The new task button is the blue of "Inbox" (R79) in any appearance, with a shadow. In the panel of lists the rows have no settings icon: the settings of "Inbox", a list, a filter and a list from the archive are opened by a long press on the row. Under a row being swiped a coloured background with an icon is visible: green with a checkmark when swiping right, yellow with a calendar when swiping left.
- R105. Android: the settings of the open view. While "Inbox", a list or a filter is open, a "More" button stands in the top bar after the sync icon; in its menu the item "Configure" opens the same settings as a long press on the row in the panel of lists (R95). Other views, and search while it is open, have no such button.
- R160. Every app names its version and its author: "Lists 0.7.0 · Denis Evsyukov", where the name is a link to https://denis.evsyukov.org. The name is written in Latin letters in both interface languages. macOS: the standard "About Lists" panel of the app menu shows, under the version, the name as a link and the line "© 2026 Denis Evsyukov"; the settings window does not show the author. Android: the line stands at the bottom of the list of settings sections (R101), under "Import…" and "Export…"; a tap on it opens the address in the browser. Web: the line stands at the bottom of the sidebar, under "Sign out"; the link opens in a new tab. The version is the version of the build: `MARKETING_VERSION` on macOS, `versionName` on Android, the version of the server on the web.
- R161. macOS: the sidebar reads as groups (#397).
