A section heading ("Lists", "Tags", "Projects", "Filters", "Archived lists") has room above it, 14 points more than one line of its text, and its title, its "+" and its chevron stand at the bottom of the row, next to the rows they head. The sections "Lists", "Tags", "Projects" and "Filters" collapse, as on Android (R158): a click on the heading collapses the section and expands it again; the chevron at the right edge of the heading points down when the section is expanded and right when it is collapsed, and a screen reader reads the heading as a button with the state "expanded" or "collapsed". The "+" of the heading stands before the chevron and creates a list, a project or a filter whether the section is collapsed or not (R43, R127). A collapsed section shows only the row of the open view, if that view is from this section; its lists are not put in order by dragging (R123), and tasks are still dropped on the row it shows. What is collapsed is kept on the Mac and is not synced, like the text size (R35) and the spacing (R122). The text of the sidebar grows half as fast as the text of the tasks: at the size step k (R35) it is the system size times 1 + (k − 1) / 2, so that at "Regular" the sidebar has 14 points against 15 of a task and its narrow column does not look larger than the list; its icons follow its text, and its column is wider by the same factor.
- R162. Web: the look of the sidebar (#397). The built-in views have the filled icons of R79 in their colours, before the name, as in the apps; a list keeps its dot in the colour of the list; a tag and a filter have their icons without colour, and the name of a tag is written without "#". The names of all rows stand in line. Section headings are written as in the apps ("Lists", "Projects", "Filters", "Tags"), not in capitals. The settings at the bottom of the sidebar ("Appearance", "Completed tasks leave the view", "Notifications…", "API tokens…", "Import…", "Export…", "Sign out") stand under the heading "Settings", apart from the views. The choice of "Appearance" and of the time after which completed tasks leave the view stands under its label, and neither the label nor the choice is cut by the edge of the sidebar. The text of the page is 14 pixels in a window wider than 720 pixels, as close to the text of the apps as the browser allows, and 16 pixels on a narrow screen, where a smaller field would make a phone zoom in on it.

### Note in Markdown

- R48. The note of a task is read as Markdown: headings (`#` … `######` and underlined `===`, `---`), bold, italic, strikethrough (`~~`), inline code and code blocks (fenced and indented), bulleted and numbered lists with nesting, task lists (`- [ ]`, `- [x]`), quotes, a horizontal rule, links (`[text](url)`, by reference, `<url>` and a bare address with `http://` or `https://`), backslash escaping. The scheme of an address is recognised case-insensitively in a link of any form: the bare address `Https://example.org`, as the keyboard types it at the start of a sentence (R70), is a link. In the address the core hands to the app, the scheme is written in lowercase and the rest as typed: Android does not open an address with the scheme `Https`. A table is shown as a grid according to R60; its source text is shown in a monospaced font. An image `![text](url)` is shown as a link with this text; nothing is loaded. HTML is not executed and not rendered: it is shown as typed. Footnotes and formulas are not recognised.
- R49. On macOS and Android the note is formatted in place while it is typed; there is no separate preview mode. Markup characters are hidden everywhere except in the block where the cursor stands or the selection lies: there they are visible and muted. A block is a paragraph, a heading, a list item without nested items, a code block, a whole table. A lone `-` under a line of text is a started list item, not a heading underline: the line above it does not become a heading for one keystroke. In a note without focus the markup is hidden completely. The marker of a list item is shown as a dot, a number stays a number, the `>` of a quote is shown as a bar on the left. The text size and typeface (R35) remain the basis: headings and code are derived from the chosen font.
- R50. Formatting does not change the text of the note: exactly what was typed is stored, synced, sent to CalDAV and searched. The text is changed only by typing, by Return according to R52 and by toggling a checkbox according to R51.
- R51. `[ ]` and `[x]` in a task list item are shown as a checkbox. A click on macOS and a tap on Android toggle it: one character between the brackets changes in the text of the note, and the note is saved at once. The checkbox stays a checkbox in the block with the cursor too; the brackets are visible only while the cursor stands right next to them. The checkboxes of a note have nothing to do with subtasks.
- R52. Return at the end of a non-empty list item starts the next item with the same indent and marker: in a numbered list the number is one higher, in a task list the checkbox is empty. Return in the middle of an item moves the rest of the line into a new item. Return on an item without text removes its marker and leaves an empty line. In a quote Return continues `> `, and on an empty line of a quote it removes `>`. On macOS this is done by the key that starts a new line according to R39. Outside a list and a quote, and inside a code block, Return only starts a new line.
- R71. A link in a note opens with a plain click on macOS and a tap on Android, both in a note without focus and while it is being typed, including in the block with the cursor. The system opens the address: the browser or the mail app. The cursor does not move, and the text of the note does not change. A click or tap outside a link places the cursor, as in an ordinary field. The text of a link is edited by placing the cursor next to it and moving to the spot with the arrow keys. On macOS the pointer over a link is a hand; a drag started on a link selects text and does not open the link; a Shift-click extends the selection. On Android a long press on a link selects the word, as in the rest of the text; a scroll started on a link does not open it.
- R81. Android: while text is typed in the note of a task card, the line with the cursor stays visible above the keyboard. When the note is taller than the space above the keyboard, the card scrolls after the cursor: while typing, when the cursor is moved with a tap and when the keyboard appears.
- R82. macOS: the look of the card of a new task. The title is set in the same size and weight as the text of the note, and the square before it is muted. Under the title is the note with the placeholder "Notes" (the same as in the card of an open task), and under it a row of fields: what is set stands on the left as a pill with its value (start, due date, repeat, priority, tags), what is not set stands on the right as an icon without a label, thin and light (the tertiary text colour); the "#" and attachment icons are always on the right. While the picker of a field is open, its pill or icon stays in place, and it moves to the other side when the picker closes. In the quick entry window the card ends with a bar: on the left the list or project the task goes to (R129), on the right the buttons "Cancel" (the same as Esc) and "Save" (the same as Return in the title; unavailable while there is no title). The buttons of the bar are capsules with bold white text: "Save" in the accent colour, "Cancel" grey. The quick entry window has no system frame: it is drawn as a card with corners rounder than those of system windows (a radius of 16 points), and it is dragged by its background. Under the note of an empty card it keeps room for three lines, and with a longer note the window grows; the margin above the title is larger than at the sides. The main window and the menu bar window have no bar (in the main window the card is an expanded row of the list, R37), the list stands as a pill in the row of fields on the left, and the note takes as many lines as it has. A note taken from the clipboard (R58), until it is touched, is shown as a block with a background and the label "From the clipboard", no longer than three lines, with a button that removes it; a click on the block or moving into the note from the keyboard expands it into an ordinary note in full.
- R94. macOS: the look of the card of an open task. The title row has no selection background, there is no divider under the title, and there is no chevron on the right. The completion mark in all rows of the list is the size of the text, thin and muted (the tertiary text colour), and it does not carry the colour of the list. The text of the note is set in the ordinary text colour; only the placeholder "Notes" stays grey; the same holds in the card of a new task and in the quick entry window. The row of fields is arranged as in the card of a new task (R82): what is set stands on the left as a pill with its value (start, due date, repeat, reminder, priority, tags); what is not set stands on the right as a thin light icon without a label; the icons of "#", subtasks (while subtasks are not shown) and attachments are always on the right. There are no empty "Start" and "Due" pills and no "+" button. While the picker of a field is open, its pill or icon stays in place. The card is a rectangle with a corner radius of 14 points, without a visible outline, with a soft wide shadow and larger margins than before; the card of a new task opened with ⌘N has the same shape.
- R96. Android: the look of the card of an open task. The card stays a bottom sheet. The mark before the title is the same as in a row of the list (R159). The row of fields under the note is arranged as on macOS (R94): what is set stands on the left as a pill with an icon and its value (start, due date, repeat, reminder, priority, tags); what is not set stands on the right as a light icon without a label; the icons of tag, subtasks (while subtasks are not shown) and attachments are always on the right. An overdue due date of an open task is red in its pill. There are no empty "Start" and "Due" pills and no "+" button. The pill of a tag removes the tag when pressed; the other pills and icons open the picker of their value. There is no line before the subtasks. Subtasks are separated by thin lines, and the mark of a subtask is a small circle (R159); for a completed subtask it is filled, and the text is grey, not struck through.
- R99. Android: the card of an open task stretches to the full screen when its handle is dragged up. Only the height changes: the contents of the card and the look of the fields stay as before (R96), and contents that do not fit scroll. Dragging the handle down returns the card to the height of its contents and does not close it; a card at the height of its contents closes at once when dragged down. On a stretched card the handle stands below the status bar. There is no button for this; for TalkBack the handle is the action "Expand" or "Collapse". The next task opened opens as a card at the height of its contents.
- R83. The title and note of an open task that are being edited at the moment are saved not only when the field loses the keyboard but also when the app is stopped normally: on macOS on quit (⌘Q, logging out) and when the app stops being active, on Android when the app goes to the background. What was typed survives quitting and the app being unloaded from memory after going to the background. A forced stop, a crash and a power loss with the field open are not covered: they lose what was typed since the last save. The card of a new task is not saved until the task is created (R37).
- R143. Android: the title or note of an open task, when it is left (by a tap elsewhere, when the card closes or when the app goes to the background, R83), is saved only if something was typed in the field while the cursor was in it (#320). An edit from another device that arrived through sync while the cursor is in a field where nothing was typed stays after leaving the field and is visible in it. If something was typed in the field, the typed text is saved and replaces the arrived one: the last write wins, as for any field (S2 in [sync.md](sync.md)).
- R53. In the web interface a note outside editing is shown formatted, without markup characters; a click on it opens an edit field with the source text, and losing focus saves the note and returns the formatted look. Checkboxes do not toggle in the web interface. The text of the note reaches the page only as text, never as page markup; a link leads to its address only with the scheme `http`, `https` or `mailto`, and others are shown as text.
- R60. A table in a note is shown as a grid: columns are aligned to the width of the longest cell, the header row is emphasized, the row of hyphens is not shown, the alignment of a column (`:---`, `:---:`, `---:`) is respected, and formatting inside a cell (bold, code, link) is kept. A short row is padded with empty cells, and extra cells of a long row are not shown. The text of the note does not change because of this (R50). In the web interface the grid is a table in place of the source text while the note is not open for editing; a table wider than the note wraps text inside its cells, and when that is not enough, it scrolls sideways. On macOS and Android the grid is shown outside the block with the cursor: the cursor in any row of the table returns its source text in a monospaced font, and moving the cursor away returns the grid. There the grid does not wrap across lines, so a table wider than the note stays as source text; a table inside a list item or a quote also stays as source text. Tab does not move between cells.

### Input and editing on macOS

- R37. ⌘N and the "+" button at the top of the window open the card of a new task as a row of the list, at the place where the task will stand (R130), with all fields at once: title, note, start date, due date, repeat, priority, tags, list. The keyboard goes straight to the title of the card, including when the quick entry window was used just before. Return in the title and ⌘Return from any field of the card create the task if it has a title. Esc closes the card and saves the task the same way as Return in the title: with a title the task is created, without a title nothing is created and the chosen files are discarded; the same in the note of the card of a new task, in the subtask field and on its chips. The card has no cancel: a task created by mistake is deleted, and ⌘Z right after creation moves it to the Trash. This is the rule of the card in the list; the quick entry window has its own rules (R114). Esc with the date, repeat or tag picker open closes only the picker (R62). There is no separate input row at the bottom of the list. The same card stands in the quick entry window and in the menu bar window, but leaving it works differently there (R114).
- R114. macOS: the quick entry window is a window with a different purpose than the card of a new task in the list, and its requirements are recorded separately, here. It is opened from any app to write something down quickly and get back to work, so it has a cancel: Esc and the "Cancel" button close the window and discard what was typed, with or without a title, together with the chosen files and the typed subtasks; no task is created. ⌘Return from any field and the "Save" button create the task if it has a title, and close the window; Return in the title does the same. Esc with the date, repeat or tag picker open closes only the picker (R62). The same holds in the menu bar window (R24). The rest of this window (the look of the card, the note from the clipboard, subtasks) is described in R82, R58 and R113.
- R138. macOS: what is typed in the menu bar window (R24) stays while the window is open, whatever changes in the tasks: completing a task from the "Today" list in the same window, an edit in another window, sync (#319). The list of today's tasks under the card is updated meanwhile.
- R38. An open task is outlined with a frame. Dates that are not set stand in the row of fields as icons (R94, R96); subtasks are collapsed into one line "Subtasks N/M", and a task without subtasks does not mention them at all: the field for the first one is opened by the subtasks icon in the row of fields or by the "Add subtask" item of the context menu. In a project the subtasks are always expanded. In the main window at most one card is open. Opening another task and moving the selection to another row, by a click or with the arrow keys, collapse the open task; the edits of the title and note are saved. A subtask in the card is a row with a mark and a title (R10); it has no card of its own. The title is edited in the row: on macOS with Enter and with a double click, on Android with a tap on the title; while it is edited, its text is selected and copied as in any text field. The task stays expanded while its subtask is selected. macOS: when the card opens at the bottom edge of the window, the list scrolls so that the card is visible in full. The context menu over a subtask is the menu of that subtask, and it has only what applies to it: rename, complete, "Won't do", delete. The card of a new task collapses the open task, and opening a task closes the card of a new task the same way as Esc.
- R39. The settings choose what Return does in a note: it starts a new line (Esc ends editing) or ends editing (⌥Return starts a new line). The default is a new line. ⌘S and ⌘D open the start date and due date pickers from any field of the card; in the calendar the arrow keys work and Return confirms, also when the keyboard is in the time field. In an open date picker (start date, due date and reminder) common choices take one key: T is today, M is tomorrow, W is in a week, a digit from 1 to 9 is that many days ahead; each of them writes the date and closes the picker. "+" and "−" move the day selected in the calendar one day forward and back and do not close the picker. ⌫ removes the date if there was one and closes the picker. A key is recognised by its place on the keyboard, not by its character, so the same keys work in the Russian layout; digits, "+" and "−" also work on the numeric keypad. While the keyboard is in the time field, these keys go to it. The keys work however the picker was opened: ⌘S or ⌘D, a click on a chip, Space on a chip. The buttons of the picker show their keys next to them.
- R40. The settings choose the default list for new tasks: "Inbox", the list used last, or a specific list. It applies in quick entry, to the `lists://add` link, in the "Services" menu and in views without a list of their own ("Today", "Upcoming", a tag), and on macOS also as the initial choice in "Share"; the extension does not know the list used last. The choice is synced (S32 in [sync.md](sync.md)); which list was used last, each device remembers by itself. If the chosen list is archived or deleted, if the hidden "Inbox" is chosen (R121), or if the device does not yet remember the list used last, the task goes to "Inbox", and while "Inbox" is hidden, to the first list in order that is not archived; if there is none, to "Inbox". Import (R121) does not know the list used last and with this choice places tasks by the same rule. Before #255 the choice was stored on the device: the first launch of the new version writes it to the shared settings if their field is still empty and the choice is not "Inbox".
- R41. Recognition of dates, priority, tags and list in the title is turned off in the settings; then the title is saved as typed. When recognition is on, a value set by a field of the card wins over one recognised in the text; a due date and priority supplied by the list (R2, R75) give way to the value from the text.
- R75. A view gives a new task no due date, no start date and no tags: a task created in "Today" does not get a due date from it, and one created in a tag view does not get that tag. From the place of creation the task takes only the list (the open one, or, where there is no list of its own, the one per R40) and the parent in a project; the quick entry window also takes the note from the clipboard (R58). This applies to the card of a new task on macOS and Android and to the "New task" row in the web interface. The defaults of the list (R2) stay, and the card of a new task on macOS and Android shows them before saving: the priority and the due date "today" stand at their icons and change together with the list chosen in the card. The due date of the list is shown while no start date is set. A supplied value is changed and removed the same way as a chosen one; a removed value is not given to the task by the list. While a supplied value is untouched, a value from the title wins over it (R41), and that value stands at the icon.
- R130. The place of a new task, on all platforms (#308, after the model of Things). A task created from a list stands first in its list, and in a project view first among the tasks of the project; on macOS, when a task of the same list is selected, right under it, and when a subtask is selected, under its parent. This is where it stands with manual order; with another sorting (R2) and in views with their own order ("Today", "Upcoming", a tag, a filter) it stands where the sorting puts it. Android and the web interface have no single selection, and the task stands first. The quick entry window, the menu bar window, the "Services" menu, the `lists://add` link and a task from another app put the task first in its list. Subtasks typed in the card (R10, R113) are added at the end, and a copy of a task stands after the original, as before.
- R42. Android: the quick entry window has the note, start date, due date, repeat and priority; in the editor the start date and due date are always visible, and the subtask field appears on request; the default list, turning recognition off and several reminders are set up the same way as on macOS.
- R113. The card of a new task on macOS and Android lets subtasks be typed before the task is saved; the same holds in the quick entry window on both platforms (R82, R64). In the row of fields on the right stands the subtasks icon, as in the card of an open task (R94, R96); pressing it places the field for the first subtask under the row of fields. A typed subtask is a row with a title (R10): Return on macOS and "Done" on Android add it and leave the cursor in the field for the next one; a row without a title is not added; a typed row can be corrected and removed before saving. The title of a subtask is taken as typed, and the quick entry line is not parsed for it (R23). Saving the card creates the task and its subtasks in the typed order; on macOS Esc also saves the card (R37), on Android closing without saving creates neither the task nor the subtasks (R97). What is typed in the subtask field and not yet added becomes the last subtask on saving. In a project view the new task is a task of the project (R31), and its subtasks are typed the same way. A card without a title creates nothing, even if subtasks are typed. Android: a card closed without saving comes back with the "Restore" button together with the typed subtasks (R97), and what the card keeps on screen rotation and window recreation, it keeps for the subtasks as well. The web interface does not have this.
- R43. A list and a filter are created with the "+" button at the "Lists" and "Filters" headings in the macOS sidebar (the "Filters" section is visible even when empty) and from the File menu.
- R44. macOS: Return on the selected row opens the card and places the cursor at the end of the title; Tab moves from the title to the note. Esc in the card closes it, saves the edit and returns the keyboard to the list, and the row stays selected. The same holds when the card of the selected row is open and the keyboard is with the list: after expanding with the chevron or a click inside the card outside its fields, and also when the keyboard is with the sidebar. Return on a row with an open card closes it. A double click acts as Return. A closed row has a chevron: it expands the card and does not take the keyboard from the list. An open card has no chevron (R94): it is closed with Esc, Return on the row, opening another task or a click outside it. A click outside means a click on empty space of the list, on the heading of the view, on the sidebar or on the toolbar of the window: the card closes and saves the edit, and the row stays selected. A click on another row selects it as usual (R38, R117); the popovers of the card (date, repeat, tags) and the preview of a file do not close the card.
- R62. macOS: Tab in the note moves the keyboard to the first chip of the card; ⌥Tab inserts a tab character into the note. Tab and ⇧Tab go through the chips in the order they are shown; ⇧Tab on the first chip returns to the note, Tab on the last one goes to the title. The chip with the keyboard is outlined. Space or Return on a chip does the same as a click: it opens the date, repeat, reminder or tag picker, the priority or list menu, the subtask field, the file picker; on a tag chip it removes the tag, and the keyboard moves to the chip that took its place. Esc on a chip closes the card the same way as Esc in the note. Esc with the date, repeat or tag picker open closes only the picker and returns the keyboard to where it was before the picker (the chip or the field); the next Esc closes the card. The order is the same in an open task and in the card of a new task and does not depend on the system setting "Keyboard navigation". While the keyboard is on a chip, the keys of the list (arrows, Return, Space, delete) do not apply to the selected row.
- R45. A repeat is set when a task is created with the same picker as for an existing task: presets, a custom rule, the end. On macOS the "Repeat" pill is always visible in the card of a new task: in the main window, in the quick entry window and in the menu bar window; on Android it is in the card of a new task (R57) and in the quick entry window. ⇧⌘R on macOS opens the repeat picker from any field of the card, new or open (⌘R is taken by sync). The rule counts from the due date of the new task, and without a due date from the start date, as when a repeat is assigned to an existing task.
- R57. Android: on the main screen a task is added with the floating "New task" button (R159); there is no input row at the bottom of the screen. The button opens, as a bottom sheet, the same card of a new task that stands in the quick entry window (R64): title, note, start date, due date, repeat, priority, files, list. The list in the card comes from the open view, and where there is no list of its own, from the setting per R40. The view gives the task neither a due date nor a tag (R75): in "Today" and in a tag view the card opens without them. In a project the task becomes its subtask, and the card has no list choice. The due date and priority that the task will get from the list (R2) are shown by the card before saving per R75, including in the quick entry window. After sending, the next task opens again with the values of the chosen list. The title is parsed per R23 and R41. "Done" on the keyboard and the send button in the title row create the task and leave the card open for the next one: the fields are cleared, the chosen list and the size of the card stay, and the cursor returns to the title. "Back" and a tap outside the card close it; the card has no "Cancel" button. In "Completed", "Trash" and search there is no button.
- R58. The quick entry window opened empty takes the note from the clipboard: text or an address copied just before ends up in the note, and the cursor stands in the title. This applies to the window opened with the global hotkey on macOS and to the window opened from the app shortcut and the tile on Android; "Share", the menu of selected text, the menu bar window and the card of a new task in the main window do not read the clipboard. The same copied content is inserted once: what was shown in the window counts as used even if no task was created. Only text no longer than 2000 characters is taken, with spaces at the edges dropped; files, images and what the source marked as hidden (passwords) are skipped; longer text is not truncated but skipped entirely. The inserted text is edited like typed text; while it is unchanged, next to the note there is a button that removes it in one action. The setting "Start the note with the clipboard in quick entry" in the "New tasks" section belongs to the device, is not synced and is on by default; while it is off, the clipboard is not read at all. Android 12 and newer itself shows a message about reading the clipboard: it appears once per copied item, because the app recognises what it has already used by the description of the clipboard, without reading the contents.
- R59. Android: a dialog with input fields (settings, list, filter, tag, custom repeat) stays above the keyboard: while the keyboard is open, the dialog takes the space above it, its buttons are visible, and its contents scroll. In a low window (height under 480 dp: the quick entry window on a rotated phone or split screen, because the main window on a phone does not rotate, R77) the title, field and buttons of an ordinary dialog do not fit above the keyboard, so there such a dialog takes the full screen: the title and buttons stand in one row at the top and are visible with the keyboard open, and the contents scroll under them. The layout is chosen by the height of the window, not by the keyboard: the appearance of the keyboard does not rebuild the dialog and does not take the cursor out of the field. Dialogs without input fields (date, priority, choice from a list) stay ordinary.
- R63. Android: rotating the screen does not close the card of a new task and does not erase what was entered in it. The quick entry window and the main window on a wide display rotate; on a phone the main window stays in portrait orientation (R77). The title, note, start date, due date, repeat, priority, chosen files and list are kept, both in the card on the main screen (R57) and in the quick entry window. A dialog opened from the card or from the task editor (date, repeat, priority, list, tag) stays open; it keeps the step of the date choice, the fields of a custom repeat and the typed tag. A note taken from the clipboard (R58) stays after rotation together with the button that removes it; a removed note does not come back. In portrait orientation the card returns in the size it had before rotation (R64).
- R64. Android: the card of a new task is one card with two sizes; it also stands in the quick entry window. Compact, the default: a muted circle of the mark and the title, the note, the row of fields and a bar at the bottom. In the row of fields what is set stands on the left as a pill with an icon and its value (start, due date, repeat, priority, number of files); what is not set stands on the right as a light icon without a label; the file icon is always on the right. What is parsed from the title (R41) also stands as a pill: due date, priority, tags, list. Priority in a pill is named with a word, not with exclamation marks. In the bar, on the left is the list or project the task goes to (R129), on the right the capsule button "Save" in the blue of "Inbox" (R79); it is unavailable while there is no title. The compact card has no send button at the title. The note of the compact card takes one line while empty and grows with the text up to five lines; a longer note scrolls inside its field, the line with the cursor in it stays visible, and the card does not get taller: the title, the row of fields and the bar stay above the keyboard. Pills that lack width move to the next line of the row; the row does not scroll sideways. The card is expanded to the full screen by dragging its handle up (R97); there is no button for this. Expanded: the title row with the send button stands at the top, and under it scroll a note of several lines and the same fields as rows with labels; dragging the handle down collapses the card. Expanding and collapsing keep everything entered and leave the cursor where it was. In landscape orientation (on a phone only the quick entry window can be in it, R77) the card is always expanded and does not collapse: the compact card lacks room above the keyboard; with the keyboard open the title and the send button are visible, and the note and fields can be scrolled to. The note in the card is plain text in both sizes.
- R97. Android, the card of a new task in the main window. Closing without saving ("Back", a tap outside the card, a drag down) erases what was entered: the next "+" opens an empty card. If the closed card had something in it, the bar "Draft discarded" with the "Restore" button appears at the bottom of the screen for 3 seconds, in the same place as the bar for undoing a deletion. "Restore" opens the card with everything it had: title, note, start, due date, repeat, priority, files, typed subtasks (R113), list and the size of the card. Without a tap, what was entered is lost. "Had something" means a non-empty title or note, a set start, due date, repeat or priority, a chosen file, a typed subtask; the list and the size of the card by themselves do not count. An empty card and a card emptied after saving a task close without the bar. "+" pressed while the bar is on screen removes the bar and opens an empty card. Dragging the handle of the card up expands it (R64), dragging the handle down collapses an expanded card and does not close it; a compact card closes at once when dragged down. On an expanded card the handle stands below the status bar. The card in the quick entry window (R25) has the same handle: dragging up expands the card, dragging down collapses an expanded card and closes a compact one together with the window, like a tap outside the card; in landscape orientation, where the card is always expanded (R64), there is no handle. The quick entry window closes as before, without the bar. For TalkBack the handle is the action "Expand" or "Collapse".
- R65. Android: a swipe to the right started in a strip 56 dp wide at the left edge of the screen opens the panel of lists and does not touch the task under the finger. A swipe started to the right of the strip completes the task, as before. A tap on the mark, a long press and scrolling in the strip work as everywhere. With gesture navigation the outer part of the strip is taken by the system back gesture, and the panel gets the rest.
- R132. Android: swiping a task row takes effect only on release past the threshold (#310). The threshold is 40 % of the row width. Until the finger reaches the threshold, the area under the row is pale, and the action icon on it fades in and grows with the travel of the finger. At the threshold the area and the icon become bright, the icon gets larger, and the phone vibrates briefly; if the finger goes back before the threshold, the area turns pale again, and the vibration repeats on the next crossing of the threshold. Release past the threshold performs the action: to the right, complete the task (with the "Undo" bar, R131); to the left, the due date picker. Release before the threshold returns the row to its place and changes nothing, however fast the finger moved: a sharp flick while scrolling does not complete the task.
- R66. Android: a notification comes at the set minute: alarms are exact if the app is allowed "Alarms & reminders". On Android 13 and newer the permission is not there after installation: the notification settings then show a row saying that reminders may be late, and a tap on it opens the system screen of this permission for Lists. Without the permission alarms stay inexact: the system may show a notification later than set, with a delivery window of up to an hour. Before Android 12 the permission is not required. When the permission is granted, the alarms are set again at once as exact. When it is revoked, the system stops the app and removes its alarms; they come back inexact on the next launch of the app. All notifications of R36 become exact: reminders, due dates and the summary of the day, as well as the update of notifications at the change of day of R104, and with it the number on the icon (R106). The firmware of some manufacturers makes even such alarms inexact: on OnePlus with Android 16 an exact alarm of an app gets the same window as an inexact one, both with the permission and without battery restrictions; only an alarm set as the person's alarm clock stays exact there. R119 exists for such phones.
- R119. Android: in the settings, in the "Notifications" section (R101), under the "Show notifications" switch (R36) there is the switch "Reminders as alarms" with the hint "They come on time on phones that delay ordinary reminders. The system may show an alarm icon." It is off by default. When on, it sets every notification of R36 (reminders, due dates and the summary of the day) as the person's alarm clock: the firmware does not shift such an alarm. The system may then show the alarm icon in the status bar and the time of the nearest notification where it shows the next alarm; a tap on that time opens the app. The switch takes effect only when exact alarms are allowed (R66): without the permission alarms stay inexact. The update at the change of day (R104, R106) is not set this way. After switching, the alarms are set again at once. The setting is stored on the device and is not synced.
- R67. Android: a pull down at the top of the list starts sync at once, without waiting for the schedule. While it runs, an indicator is visible at the top of the list; a sync that started by itself does not show it. The gesture works in all views, including empty ones. While sync is not set up, there is no gesture. The result is shown by the same icon as always (R27).
- R70. Android: in fields with free text (task title, note, subtask, list name and filter name) the app asks the keyboard to start a sentence with a capital letter. The capital letter appears when this is on in the keyboard settings; with the setting off the letter stays lowercase. In the tag field, in search and in the filter fields "Tags" and "Contains" the app does not ask for a capital letter: a word for matching is typed there, not a sentence. Autocorrection and suggestions in fields with free text follow the keyboard settings; in server addresses, user name, password and token autocorrection is off.
- R74. Android: the day and time of a due date or start date are set in one opening of the date picker, in the card of R110.
- R110. Android: a date is chosen in one card, without the system date and time dialogs. The card is the same for the due date, start date and reminder: in the card of an open task, in the card of a new task (R57), in the quick entry window (R42) and after swiping a row to the left. At the top are the quick choices "Today", "Tomorrow" and "In a week": a tap saves that day with the time set in the card and closes the card. Under them is the month grid: the name of the month and the year with arrows to the neighbouring months; the week starts on the first day of the week of the interface language; today is outlined, and the selected day is filled. On opening, the day of the task is selected and its month is shown, and while there is no date, today. A tap on a day selects it and does not close the card. Under the grid is the "Time" row: it shows the time of the task, and while there is no time, "Off". A tap on the row expands the hour and minute fields under it (24-hour), and in the row itself the "Remove time" button takes the place of the time; if there was no time, expanding sets 09:00. "Done" saves the selected day, with or without time, and closes the card. "Remove" stands next to "Cancel" when the date is set, and removes the date. "Cancel" and a tap outside the card change nothing. A reminder must have a time: the time fields are expanded at once, there is no "Remove time" button, and a quick choice only selects the day and does not close the card. The card does not accept a date in words.
- R77. Android: on a phone the main window of the app stays in portrait orientation and does not follow the rotation of the device. Together with it the list, the task card, the card of a new task (R57), dialogs and the attachment preview stay portrait. The quick entry window is not locked: it opens over another app, which may be in landscape orientation. On a display whose shorter side is 600 dp or more (a tablet, an unfolded foldable phone) there is no lock, and the app rotates with the device. In split screen the system sets the orientation, and the window may turn out low. For windows that rotate or can be low, R59, R63 and R64 apply.

### Sync

- R26. The app works fully without a network and without sync set up. Edits are saved locally at once.
- R27. Sync needs no action from the user after setup. Its state is shown by one unobtrusive icon; an error does not get in the way of work.
- R29. The storage password is not written to the app database: it is kept in the system secret store (the macOS keychain, Android Keystore). The passphrase of an encrypted storage is kept the same way (S58 in [sync.md](sync.md)).
- R145. The sync settings for WebDAV and a folder (on macOS) let the storage be encrypted with a passphrase and the passphrase be changed (S54–S59, S62 in [sync.md](sync.md), #342). Under the storage password is the "Storage passphrase" field with a hint that it is needed only for an encrypted storage; it is saved together with the other settings and takes effect from the next sync. Under the fields is the "Encrypt the storage…" button, and when the storage is encrypted, "Change passphrase…". Both open a window with two passphrase fields and a warning: a forgotten passphrase cannot be recovered, the data of the storage is lost with it, and the copies on the devices remain. The confirm button is available when the fields are non-empty and match. The buttons act on the saved settings: while the form is not saved, they are absent. Encryption runs in the background; while it runs, the buttons are unavailable and the settings name what is running; when it ends, the passphrase is written to the same place as the password and appears in the field, and an error is shown under the buttons. A device without the passphrase shows the sync error of the core like any other. The web server cannot encrypt: it takes the passphrase from `LISTS_SYNC_PASSPHRASE`.
- R28. Simultaneous edits of different fields of one task on different devices are both kept. The rules are in [sync.md](sync.md).

### Signing in to the web interface

The decisions of the author of the project of 2026-10-17 are recorded in #314.

- R134. A client without a session can neither tie up the web server nor guess the password without limit. The server handles each request in a separate thread, so a slow request or an unfinished body does not hold up the others. The server itself has no read timeout: `tiny_http` does not let one be set on a connection, and a timeout on the listening socket on Linux breaks the accepting of connections; slow clients are cut off by the reverse proxy ([self-hosting.md](../self-hosting.md)). A wrong password gets the 401 response at once, without a pause. An attempt counts as failed from its start until a successful sign-in, so simultaneous attempts cannot check more passwords than allowed. After 5 failed attempts within a minute from one client the server responds 429 with the `Retry-After` header and does not check the password until the oldest of those five attempts is a minute old; a successful sign-in resets the count of the client. A client is the address of the connection, and with `LISTS_TRUST_PROXY=1` the last address in `X-Forwarded-For`, the one appended by the reverse proxy; without this header, again the address of the connection. The server remembers failures for no more than 10 000 clients, and on overflow forgets the client with the oldest last attempt. Each failed attempt is a line in the error stream with the address of the client and the number of failures within the minute; a refusal by the limit is one line, when the limit is reached; the password is not written to the log. The body of a sign-in request is no larger than 4 KB: if the `Content-Length` header is larger, the server responds 413 and does not read the body. The server remembers no more than 1000 unfinished OpenID Connect sign-ins; a new sign-in pushes out the oldest one, and whoever started that one signs in again. The session cookie carries `Secure` when `LISTS_WEB_URL` starts with `https://`.
- R135. The web server counts "today", the time of day and the end of the day by the clock of the browser, not by its own (#315). With every request the page passes the offset of its zone from UTC in minutes, in the `X-Lists-Zone` header. By it the server builds "Today", "Upcoming" and the counters, sets the date of a quick entry task with a time and no date, writes the moment of completion and keeps a completed task until midnight in the browser (R68). This way one server answers browsers in different zones. A request without the header or with an offset over 14 hours is counted by the zone of the server, as before. Sync timestamps do not depend on the zone. The offset is taken at the moment of the request, so on the day of a clock change midnight may differ from the zone of the browser by an hour.
- R144. The web server answers `GET /healthz` without signing in: code 200 and the body `ok`, with no data and no session (#321). The command `lists-web healthcheck` requests this address from the server listening on `LISTS_WEB_LISTEN` (the address `0.0.0.0` or `[::]` is replaced with loopback), waits for an answer no longer than five seconds and exits with code 0 on a 200 response, with code 1 otherwise. The Docker image calls it in `HEALTHCHECK`: the image has neither a shell nor curl.

### Notifications in the web interface

Split off from #322; the design note is in #335.

- R146. The web interface delivers the notifications of R36 through Web Push (#335). Each browser in which notifications are on is a separate device for the server, with its own R36 settings: whether to show notifications, how long before to remind of a task due at a time (at the due time, 5, 15, 30 minutes, 1 and 2 hours, a day before; several at once are allowed), at what hour to remind of a task due on a day, whether to send the summary of the day and when. The defaults are the same as on Android: a reminder at the due time, tasks due on a day at 09:00, no summary. What to send and when is computed by the core, as for the apps; every 30 seconds the server sends the notifications whose moment has come since the previous check, each one once. A notification whose moment passed while the server was not running is not sent. The text is put together by the browser at the moment of display, per R36: the title of the task and "Due: today", "Due: tomorrow, 18:30"; for the summary, "Lists" and "Tasks due today or overdue: N", as on Android. A notification with the same key replaces the previous one. A click on a notification opens the page with that task, and for the summary, "Today".
- R147. The settings of R146 open with the "Notifications…" item in the sidebar. The "Show notifications in this browser" switch asks for the permission of the browser when turned on; the "Send a test notification" button sends one notification to this browser at once. When the browser cannot get notifications, the form says why, and the switch is unavailable: the page is opened neither over HTTPS nor from `localhost`, "Notifications work over HTTPS only."; there is no Push API, "This browser cannot get notifications.", and on iPhone and iPad outside the app on the Home Screen, "On iPhone and iPad, add Lists to the Home Screen first."; the permission is denied, "The browser does not allow notifications for this site."
- R148. The subscription of a browser is kept on the server, in a file of its data folder, together with the settings of R146 and the time zone of the browser, and is not synced. The zone is kept as a name (`Europe/Moscow`) and an offset; the server converts the name to an offset on every check, so after a clock change notifications arrive at the same hour as before, even if the page has not been opened. A name the server does not know is replaced by the saved offset. On opening, the page repeats its subscription, and the server updates the zone. The content of a notification is encrypted for the browser (`aes128gcm`, RFC 8291), and the request is signed with the key of the server (VAPID, RFC 8292): the push service sees neither the title nor the due date. The server creates the key pair on its first start and keeps it in the data folder. A subscription for which the push service answered 404 or 410 is deleted by the server. Signing out of the web interface first deletes the subscription of this browser. Only an `https://` subscription address is accepted.
- R149. The page opens without a network (#335). The service worker keeps the page, the manifest and the icons; navigation to the page goes to the network first and, without an answer, to the saved copy. Responses of `/api/` are not saved. When the server is unreachable, the page shows "The server is unavailable." and a "Retry" button, not an empty screen and not old data.

### API tokens

Split from #322, described in #344.

- R150. Scripts, Shortcuts, Tasker and Raycast reach the web server with a token, not with the cookie of a browser (#344). Tokens are created in the web interface with the "API tokens…" item of the sidebar: a token has a name and one of two scopes, "Read" or "Read and write". A token is shown once, when it is created, with a hint to copy it now. The server keeps not the token itself but its SHA-256, together with the name, the scope, the time of creation and the time of last use to the minute, in the file `api-tokens.json` of its data folder, readable only by the owner. Tokens survive a restart of the server and are not synced. A token is revoked in the same list; a revoked token stops working at once.
- R151. The token is passed in the header `Authorization: Bearer lists_…` with any request to `/api/`. A request with a token needs no cookie, and the header `X-Lists` is not required of it. A "Read" token allows only `GET` and `HEAD`; the server answers 403 to everything else. No scope allows creating and revoking tokens, signing out or subscribing to notifications (R146): these stay with the browser session, and the server answers 403 to such requests made with a token. Each token has its own undo (R131). A wrong or revoked token gets 401.
- R152. One token makes at most 600 requests a minute; beyond that the server answers 429 with `Retry-After`. A wrong token counts as a failed sign-in attempt under R134, and after five in a minute the client gets 429 for both passwords and tokens. Unlike a password, an attempt with a token is not counted in advance: a token is 256 random bits, and the limit here protects against noise, not against guessing. Neither the token nor its hash goes into the server log; there the token is referred to by its name.

### Export

Split from #322, described in #349.

- R155. Data is exported to JSON (#349): everything or one list. The file `Lists-<date>.json` is in the format described in [json-format.md](../json-format.md): lists with their settings, tasks with all fields (dates, priority, tags, repeat as a rule, reminder, note, subtasks, completion and "won't do", the project flag), saved filters (only in an export of everything) and the names of attachments with size and SHA-256. The content of attachments is not in the file. The trash is not exported; a record of a completed repeat is exported as a completed task. Import reads such a file (I11 in [import.md](import.md)). macOS: "File → Export…"; Android: "Export" in the settings; web: "Export…" in the sidebar, and the file is downloaded. Everywhere the format is chosen, and "Everything" or the current list.
- R156. Data is exported to Markdown, for reading (#349): the heading `# Lists, <date>`, one `##` section per list, tasks as a to-do list `- [ ]` and `- [x]` with subtasks indented under the task. After the title, following a dash, come the markers: project, "won't do", start, due date, reminder, repeat, priority, tags `#tag`, number of attachments. The note goes in indented lines under the task. The title of a "won't do" task is struck through. Such a file is not read back.
- R157. Android: tasks and lists are reordered by dragging (#373). A long press on a task row in Inbox and in a list sorted manually (R2) lifts the row with a short vibration; the row follows the finger, the others make room, and the released row takes its new place — right after the row above it, or at the top if there is none above it. A row held at the top or bottom edge scrolls the list, the faster the closer it is to the edge. A list row in the "Lists" section of the panel is reordered the same way. The new order is written as on macOS (`moveTask`, `moveList`), synced and undone (R131). A long press released in place opens the same as before: the task menu or the list settings; a screen reader opens them with a long press, as before. In views with a computed order ("Today", "Upcoming", "All", tags, filters, search), in a list with another sort and in selection mode (R109), rows are not dragged, and a long press opens the menu at once. Swiping a row (R132) works everywhere as before.
- R158. Android: the sections "Lists", "Projects", "Filters" and "Tags" of the panel of lists collapse (#380, decision #368 in `docs/android-visual-direction.md`). A tap on the heading of a section collapses it and expands it again; the heading has a chevron: down when expanded, right when collapsed, and a screen reader reads it as a button with the state "expanded" or "collapsed". A collapsed section shows only the row of the open view, if that view is from this section, and has no "New list", "New project", "New filter" rows; lists of a collapsed section are not dragged (R157). What is collapsed is kept on the device and is not synced, like the text size (R100) and the spacing (R122). Rows of the panel follow the spacing of R122: 48, 52 and 56 dp for "Compact", "Normal" and "Relaxed".
- R159. Android: the look of the screen follows direction B from #368 — selective Material 3 Expressive (#387, decision of the author of the project of 2026-10-18 in `docs/android-visual-direction.md`); macOS stays with R72. The rows of a group stand in a shared rounded container (corner 24 dp) of the surface colour on a screen background of another tone, with thin lines between rows. The completion mark is a 20 dp circle; for a completed task it is filled with the primary colour of the theme with a check mark of the surface colour, for a "Won't do" task it is filled with a muted colour with a cross; the title of a completed task is muted and not struck through. The priority bar is 4 dp wide and 36 dp high, with rounded ends. The task title is set in medium weight. Under the title, when there is something to show, comes a second line: the list name in the list colour (in views where lists are mixed, as in R72), the project after a dash, and the due date as a capsule chip; an overdue due date has a red chip. There is no due date at the right edge of the row. A task without a list and due date in the row takes one line of text. The group heading is a tonal capsule; "Overdue" is red. The large view title is larger and bolder, and its icon stands on a rounded tile of the icon colour. The new task button is wide, with a "+" icon and the label "New task", corner 16 dp. The bottom sheets of the task card and of the card of a new task have a top corner of 28 dp; field chips are 36 dp high with a 12 dp corner, and the due date chip is tonal. Rows of the panel of lists have a 16 dp corner, the row icon stands on a tile of the icon colour; the corner of the panel itself is 28 dp. Settings sections stand in a rounded container, each with its icon on a tonal tile.

### Backups

The model is the backups of 2Do (documentation: <https://www.2doapp.com/docs/macos/backups/>, <https://www.2doapp.com/docs/android/backups>); the decisions of the author of the project of 2026-10-09 are recorded in #140. Backups exist in the apps for macOS and Android; the web server has none, there the data volume is copied, except for the backup before a database migration (R92).

- R87. A backup is one file with the extension `.listsbackup`: a zip archive holding `manifest.json` (format number, time of creation, number of fields and attachments), the SQLite database `lists.sqlite` with all fields of all records together with their stamps ([sync.md](sync.md)), and the content of attachments that is on the device, in `blobs/<sha256>`. A backup includes tasks, lists, tags, projects, filters, attachment records, the trash, "Completed" and the shared settings (S32). It does not include: where and how the device syncs, passwords and tokens, the device identifier, the settings of the device itself (notifications, appearance, default list, backup schedule), and the content of attachments that is not on the device. The file name carries the time of creation: `Lists_(2026-10-09-11_15_05).listsbackup`.
- R88. Backups are kept in the `backups` folder inside the data folder of the app. Backup settings belong to the device and are not synced: "Create backups" — "Never", "Every 24 hours", "Every 48 hours", every 24 hours by default; "Keep at most" — 5, 10, 20 or 30, 5 by default. An automatic backup is created when the set time has passed since the last automatic one: the app checks this on start and with every scheduled sync, and on Android also with a separate background job once an hour that needs no network, so a backup appears only on a device where the app runs or its background job runs. Without a network and with the app closed, an automatic backup on Android is still created on time (#320). After each new backup, automatic or manual, the oldest ones beyond the set number are deleted. On a device that has neither tasks nor lists yet, no automatic backup is created.
- R89. The settings have a "Backups" section: the schedule, the number of backups to keep, "Create backup now", the list of backups from newest to oldest with date and size, "Restore from file…". A backup in the list has three actions: "Restore…", "Save a copy…" — write the file where the user points — and "Delete". The app does not count or delete backups outside the app.
- R90. Restoring replaces all data of the app with the content of the backup and runs after a confirmation that says so. Before the replacement the app itself creates a backup of the current state; if it cannot be created, restoring does not start. The data changes in one write: on a failure the previous state remains. The content of attachments from the backup is added to what is on the device; a file whose content does not match its name is not taken. The device settings and the sync settings stay as they were. A file that is not a backup of the app, a backup with an unknown format number and a damaged archive are rejected with a clear error, and the data does not change. A backup can be restored on another device and in a clean install. What happens after restoring with sync on is S40 in [sync.md](sync.md).
- R92. Before a database migration the app creates a backup (R87). A migration here is a change to the field table or the service tables that rewrites what they hold; the schema of these tables has a version number, kept in the database, and the backup is created when the app opens a database with a number lower than its own. A database created before the number appeared counts as a database of version one. The backup is an ordinary one: it lies in the `backups` folder, is visible in the list of backups in the settings and takes a place in the rotation (R88) — after it the oldest backups beyond the set number are deleted; it does not depend on the backup schedule and is created even when automatic backups are off. If the backup cannot be created, the migration does not start, the database is not opened and the data stays as it was. A new install and a database into which nothing has been written yet get no backup. Rebuilding derived tables does not cause a backup: they are built from the fields and lose nothing. Adding a column or a table does not count as a migration. A database left by a newer version of the app gets no backup and keeps its number. The backup is created by the core, so on the web server it also appears in the `backups` folder of the data volume; there is no settings section for backups there, and such a backup is restored by the app for macOS or Android.
- R153. With WebDAV sync or sync through a folder, backups are also kept in the storage (#347), under the rules S60 and S61 in [sync.md](sync.md). Restoring from such a backup returns attachments as "not downloaded yet", and their content comes from the storage (S34). Content that the storage deleted as unneeded (S47, S48) after the backup was created is not returned.
- R154. In the "Backups" section (R89) on macOS and Android there is a switch "Keep copies in the sync storage". It is there only with WebDAV and a folder and is on by default. The switch belongs to the device and is not synced. Under it is the time of the last copy of this device in the storage, and after a failed upload, its reason. Below stands the list "In the sync storage": copies of all devices, from newest to oldest, with date; copies of this device are marked "this device", on macOS "this Mac". There is no size in the list: the storage lists file names, and the size of each would cost one more request. A copy in the list has the action "Restore…": the copy is downloaded and restored under R90, with a backup of the current state and a confirmation.

## Interface

Principles by which decisions about screens are made:

1. **The list is the main screen; the editor does not open in a separate window.** On macOS a task expands in place, on Android it opens as a bottom sheet over the list: title, note and a row of chips (due date, repeat, priority, tags, attachments). Empty chips take no space: a field that is not set stands as a thin icon without a label (R94, R96).
2. **One action, one obvious gesture.** Complete: the mark on the left; "won't do": an item of the task menu (R69); open: a tap; move: dragging. Swiping on Android past the threshold (R132): right to complete, left for the due date, right from the left edge of the screen for the panel of lists (R65), down at the top of the list for sync (R67).
3. **The context menu has no more than seven items:** due date (today, tomorrow, pick), priority, move to list, add subtask, duplicate, delete. The rest lives in the expanded task.
4. **Ask nothing in advance.** A new task is created with one line and the Enter key; all fields are optional and are filled in later.
5. **No modal confirmations for what can be reversed.** Any action on a task can be undone (R131), and completion also by tapping the mark again while the task is still in place (R68); only emptying the trash and clearing "Completed" have a confirmation, because they cannot be reversed.
6. **On macOS the keyboard covers everything:** ⌘N — new task, arrows — selection, Enter — expand and edit the title, Esc — close the card, Space or ⌘Enter — complete, ⌘T — due today, ⌘1…⌘6 — views, ⌘F — search, ⌘Z — undo, ⌃⌥Space — quick entry from any app (the shortcut is changed in the settings).
7. **Colour carries meaning.** Colour belongs to a list; red marks only an overdue due date. Priority is shown by a sign at the mark, not by filling the row. There are two colours not from a list, and neither stands in a task row: the icons of the built-in views (R79), in fixed colours that tell the views apart, none of them red, and the calendar bar of an event in "Today" (R78), in a muted block above the tasks.

macOS: the sidebar (views, lists, tags) and the task list. There is no third column. The mark in a task row stands at the middle of the row height (R72), in an open card at the middle of the first line of the title: there the middle of the mark matches the middle of a capital letter at any size and weight of the text (R35).
Android: one screen with the task list, a slide-out panel with the lists, and a floating "New task" button that opens the card of a new task as a bottom sheet (R57); the card is compact, with field icons, and expands to full screen (R64).

## Verification scenarios

Scenarios of the core are checked by tests in `core/tests/`; test names match the requirement identifiers where possible.

- R5: a task with a start date of tomorrow → the "Today" query does not contain it; after the date changes to today it does.
- R11: a task with two subtasks → complete the parent → both subtasks are completed. One subtask completed beforehand → complete the parent → undo → the parent and the second subtask are open, the first stays completed.
- R10: task "a" → type "b" under it → "a" has subtask "b". Give "b" a due date, note, priority or tag → rejected. Add a subtask under "b" → rejected. Move "b" to another list or under another task, move task "c" under "a" → rejected. Project "p" with task "t" due tomorrow → the due date stays, "t" is visible in "Upcoming"; under "t" subtask "u" → it has only a title. Turn "p" back into a task → rejected.
- R112: the data has task "a", under it "b" due today with a note, a tag and an attachment, under "b" — "c" → after normalisation the subtasks of "a" are "b", "c" in this order; "b" has no due date, note, tag or attachment, and it is not in "Today". The same after a sync with a device that sent such data, after an import and after an edit by another CalDAV client. A project task with a due date stays as it was.
- R22: task "buy" with subtasks "milk" and "bread" → search "milk" → the results have "buy", with "milk" under it; there is no "bread" row and no separate "milk" row.
- R113: the card of a new task, title "trip", the subtasks icon → type "билеты завтра" (tickets tomorrow), Return, "hotel" → save → task "trip" with subtasks "билеты завтра" and "hotel" in this order, the subtasks have no due date. macOS: the same, but Esc instead of ⌘Return → the same task with the same subtasks; ⌘Z → the task is in the trash. A card without a title with a typed subtask, Esc → nothing is created. Android: type a title and a subtask, close the card with the "Back" button, tap "Restore" → the title and the subtask are in place.
- R17: task "every week" due 2026-10-05 → complete → due 2026-10-12, the task is active, "Completed" has one record dated 2026-10-05.
- R128: task "every week" due 2026-10-05 19:00, start 2026-10-03, reminder at 18:45 and subtasks "a", completed at 9:00, and "b" → complete → undo → due date, start and reminder as before, "a" completed at 9:00, "b" open, "Completed" empty. Complete again → change the due date to 2026-10-20 → undo → due 2026-10-20, the record in "Completed" stays. A tap on the mark of the record in "Today" within the first 5 seconds → the same as undo.
- R131: task "a" due tomorrow, third in the list "Home" → due today → move to "Work" → undo → "a" is third in "Home", due today → undo → due tomorrow → undo → nothing changes. "a", "b", "c" → due in a week in one action → on another device "b" gets due 2026-11-01, sync → undo → "a" and "c" have their previous due date, "b" has 2026-11-01. Create a task → undo → the task is in the trash. Title "a" → "ab" → "abc" → undo → "a". Restart → undo → nothing changes. Web: session A sets a due date, session B undoes → nothing changes; session A undoes → the previous due date.
- R68, repeat: setting "5 seconds", "Today" has "a", "every day" due today, "c" → complete "every day" at 10:00 → at 10:00:04 "Today" shows "a", "every day" (completed), "c", and "Upcoming" has "every day" due tomorrow; the list of the task has the open "every day" and next to it the completed record → at 10:00:06 the record is not in "Today" or in the list, it is in "Completed".
- R18: "every month on the 31st" due 2026-01-31 → complete → due 2026-02-28.
- R23: the line "купить молоко завтра 18:30 !! #дом @Покупки" (buy milk tomorrow 18:30 !! #home @Shopping) → title "купить молоко" (buy milk), due tomorrow 18:30, priority medium, tag "дом" (home), list "Покупки" (Shopping).
- R68, precision: setting "15 seconds", a task completed at 10:00:40 → at 10:00:54 it is in place, at 10:00:56 it is gone.
- R68, until the end of the day: setting "until the end of the day", a task completed at 10:00 → at 23:59 it is in place, at 00:00 of the next day it is gone and is in "Completed". A task completed yesterday at 23:50 is not in view today, even if the setting was chosen only today.
- R68: at 10:00 "Today" has "a", "b", "c" → complete "b" → at 10:00:04 the view shows "a", "b" (completed), "c", the "Today" count is two → at 10:00:06 "b" is gone, it is in "Completed". The same with the setting "at once": "b" disappears at 10:00:00. In a list that shows completed tasks, "b" stands between "a" and "c" at 10:00:04, and after "c" at 10:00:06.
- R46: three tasks completed on 1 August, 20 September and 5 October, a repeating task has a repeat record from 1 August, an open task has a completed subtask, one task is in the trash → clear completed before 5 September → the task and the record from 1 August have left "Completed", the rest is in place, the trash has not changed; clear everything → "Completed" is empty, the open task with its subtask and the trash are intact.
- R69: Inbox has "a", "b", "c" → "won't do" for "b" → "b" is in place with a cross in the mark, the Inbox count is two; after the R68 time it is not in Inbox, it is in "Completed" and in "Won't do"; the completed "a" is in "Completed" but not in "Won't do". Return "b" to work → it is in Inbox again and in neither log.
- R69: a task with three subtasks, one completed → "won't do" for the parent → the two open subtasks get the same outcome, the completed one stays completed → undo → the parent and the two subtasks are open, the completed one stays completed.
- R38, R69: macOS, a task with two subtasks is open → "Won't do" in the context menu over the first subtask → only it is closed, the parent and the second subtask are open.
- R69, R17: task "every week" due 2026-10-05 → "won't do" → due 2026-10-12, the task is open, "Won't do" has one record dated 2026-10-05.
- R69, R30: a project has three tasks, two completed, one "won't do" → the counter is 2/2.
- R120: Inbox has tasks "a" and "b", the Inbox count is 2 → make "b" a project → Inbox has only "a", count 1, "b" is in the sidebar. Give "b" a due date of today and the tag "home" → it is not in "Today", "All" or the view of the tag "home", the counts of "Today" and the tag are 0; searching "b" finds the project. Delete "b" → it is in the trash; restore it. Turn the empty project "b" back into a task → Inbox has "a" and "b" in the previous order, count 2. Project "p" with task "t" due today → "Today" has "t" and not "p". Complete "t", then "p" → "p" is not in Inbox, not even with completed tasks shown; it is in "Completed".
- R125: today is 5 October. Project "Renovation" due 10 October, with tasks "a" without dates and "b" due 20 October → "Upcoming" has "a" on 10 October, "b" on 20 October; the due date in the row of "a" is muted and has the project icon, that of "b" is ordinary. Project due 3 October → "a" is in "Today" among the overdue, the overdue count is 1, the filter "Overdue" shows "a"; the data of "a" has not received a due date. Project start 8 October → "a" is not in "Today", it is in "Upcoming"; a task "c" without dates in the same project stays without dates while the project has no due date. The reminder for "a" comes at the due date of the project.
- R127: the list "Work" is open, with "due today" for new tasks → "New project" with the title "Vacation" → the sidebar has the project "Vacation" 0/0, it is in the list "Work", without a due date or priority, and its view is open. In "Today" → "New project" → the project is in the default list (R40). An empty title does not create a project.
- R69, R33: a filter with the state "completed" shows only the completed task, with the state "won't do" only such a task, with the state "all" both and the open ones.
- R69, R46: a completed task and a "won't do" task closed a month ago → clear "Completed" → both logs are empty.
- R72: the list has task "a" with the note "buy", a repeat, an attachment, a subtask and the tag "home", and task "b" without the other fields → "a" has four signs after the title without text or numbers, and the text "buy" and "#home" are not in the row; "b" has nothing after the title, both rows are of the same height. Erase the note of "a" → three signs. In "Today" a task due today → the right side is empty; due today at 17:00 → "17:00" on the right; due yesterday → "Yesterday" in red on the right. The same task due today in its own list → "Today" on the right. The list "Home" has the task "windows" and the project "renovation" with the task "tiles"; Inbox has the task "bread" and the project "vacation" with the task "tickets", all due today → in "Today" under the title: "windows" has "Home" in the list colour, "tiles" has "Home – renovation", "bread" has "Inbox", "tickets" has "Inbox – vacation"; the mark stands at the middle of the two lines. In the list "Home" for "windows" and in Inbox for "bread" there is no list name, the row takes one line of text. Give "windows" high priority and "bread" low → after the mark "windows" has a red bar, "bread" a blue one, there are no "!" signs; the titles "windows", "bread" and of a task without priority start on the same vertical line.
- R84: task "a" with a start date of yesterday and no due date, task "b" due yesterday → in "Today" "b" stands under "Overdue", "a" under "Today", the "Today" count is red. Complete "b" → there are no group headings, "a" is in place, the count is not red.
- R103: macOS, a new install, task "a" due yesterday and task "b" due today → the Dock icon shows "2". In the settings, section "Notifications", "Count on the icon" → "Overdue tasks" → "1"; complete "a" → no number; "Do not show" → no number; "Today" → "1". Quit and open the app → the choice is the same. The app is hidden, a task due today is added through the quick entry window → the number has grown by one.
- R36: a new install, a task due tomorrow at 10:00 → one notification is scheduled, at 10:00. The device has the saved choice "15 minutes before", the app is updated → the choice is "At the due time", the notification is at 10:00; choose "15 minutes before" again, restart → the choice is "15 minutes before", the notification is at 9:45. Saved choices "15 minutes before" and "an hour before", update → the choice is as before. Saved "off", update → "off". A task due tomorrow, notifications scheduled today; the device clock is moved to tomorrow, 08:59 → at 9:00 the notification "Due: today", not "Due: tomorrow".
- R104: Android, a new install → in the "Notifications" section the switch "Today's tasks in the notification shade" is off, there is no notification. Tasks "a" due yesterday and "b" due today, switch it on → the shade has a notification of the "Quick add" channel: "Today: 2", rows "a" and "b", the button "New task". Tap the button → the quick entry window is open; create "в сегодня" (c today) in it → "Today: 3". Complete "a" → "Today: 2", no row "a". Seven tasks for today → five titles and "2 more". Swipe the notification away → it is in the shade again. Restart the device without opening the app → the notification is in the shade. Switch it off → no notification.
- R106: Android, a new install → in the "Notifications" section "Count on the icon" is "Do not show". Tasks "a" due yesterday and "b" due today, the notification with tasks is on, choose "Today" → the shade has one persistent Lists notification, "Today: 2" with the number 2, the launcher icon has a badge. "Overdue tasks" → the same notification, "Overdue: 1" with the number 1. Complete "a" → "Today: 1", no number and no badge. "Do not show" → "Today: 1", no badge. Switch off the notification with tasks while "Today" is chosen → no notification and no badge, a caption under the choice. Update from a version with two notifications where only the "Today" count was on → the shade has one notification with tasks and the number, and no notification of the "Count on the icon" channel.
- R85: a file "Image" with JPEG content is attached to a task → in the card it is shown as "Image.jpg" with a thumbnail and opens as an image; a file "blob" with unknown content is shown as "blob".

- R88: schedule "Every 24 hours", keep 5 → the app runs for six days → the list has 5 backups, the oldest is gone. Schedule "Never" → no new backups appear. "Create backup now" with five backups → the list has 5, the oldest is deleted. Android, schedule "Every 24 hours", network off, the app closed for two days → two automatic backups have appeared.
- R93: a new install without tasks → the sidebar has "Inbox", "Today", "Upcoming", "All"; there are no "Completed", "Won't do" and "Trash" rows, and ⌘5 does nothing. Undo → "a" and "p" in Inbox, Inbox is shown, R40 as before. Hide without moving → "a" in Inbox, the Inbox row with count 1; move "a" to "Home" → no row. Import of a task without a list while Inbox is hidden → it is in "Home". With no lists except Inbox and "Old" → hiding is not possible.
- R122: macOS, a new install, the text size "Small" → in Inbox task rows are 30 points each; "Spacing between tasks" — "Compact" → 26 at once, without a restart; "Relaxed" → 40; in "Today" a row with a list name is 15 points taller than an Inbox row at any step. Quit and open the app → the step is the same. Open a task card → its look does not depend on the step. Android → rows are 64 dp; "Compact" → 56, "Relaxed" → 72; the mark can be tapped at any step.

The list "Work" → a task without a due date takes one line of text, a task due in a week takes two, with a due date capsule. At the bottom right, the "New task" button with a label.

- R92: the device has tasks and a list; a version of the app that changes the schema of the field table is installed → on the first start a backup with the previous data appears in the `backups` folder, after it the database is moved to the new schema, the tasks are in place. Keep 5, there are already five backups → after the start of the new version there are five backups, the oldest is gone.

The content of "b.pdf" has not arrived yet but is in the storage → the row shows the download, the file is downloaded, the folder has all three. The content of "b.pdf" is not in the storage either → the message names "b.pdf", the folder has "a.txt" and "c.png". Android: "Share all" → the system share sheet with three files, the first named by its own name.

"Remove" → no due date. A task without a due date → "Cancel" → no due date.
